<!-- AUG:ibrahim-2015 lecture 1 -->

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. [Arabic recitation of prayer]. La junaha. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Yes? Correct. La junaha. Right.
Ikhwan-i-Mu’minin, for a short while, this lesson will take place between Maghrib and ‘Isha. For the four or five days that I am here. After that, whatever arrangement is made here… Hazrat Qibla will take the lesson at 6 o'clock. His health is not good today, that's why he didn't take it. From tomorrow, Insha’Allah, he will take it.

After ‘Isha also, the students who wish to study can go to Mulla Is’haq Sahib… to Hazratullah or to anyone else they wish to study with. With Mulla Sa’dullah Sahib. They can study.

This is *Rawdat al-Mustafid*, which is taught every year. Hazrat Zayni Sahib Muqaddas gave the order to translate it. [unclear] a little. And before that, this *ta’wil* has been explained to us, of the glorious Qur’an. In fact, it was written 150-200 years ago, but because it wasn't in Urdu, it wasn't read. By the order of Hazrat Zayni Sahib, Mulla Amir Sahib, the ‘Amil of the time there in Surat, translated it, and he did so in a fluent translation. And in this regard, it has been arranged that there are several parts of *Rawdat al-Mustafid*, and approximately up to Surah al-‘Ankabut, that is, Surah Yasin, the *siparah* is ‘Ankabut. Up to there, its translation has been done. And after that, the translation has not been done.

So we have started this before, from Surah al-Fatiha, and it should be so, that it starts from Surah al-Fatiha. But that book is not available, so the second *juz’*, that is, we are starting from Surah al-Baqarah. And wherever the glorious Qur’an is read from, there is blessing. In the same way, wherever its *ta’wil* is explained from, it is worthy of respect.

This is Surah al-Baqarah, it is the second *juz’*, and here, which verse is this? 100… 134? Page 134. The verse is around 120. It's not printed, anyway. It is the second *parah*. Surah al-Baqarah is the longest Surah of the glorious Qur’an.

And the things that have come in it, their meaning in *ta’wil* is different, and in the *zahir* (exoteric), the meaning is different. And if this were not so, every person would become a scholar. And the one who reads the translation would call himself an ‘alim. But the decision of Allah Ta'ala is that whomever He gives, He knows whether that person is worthy of it or not. In this regard, the *ta’wil* that has been explained to us, from it, the *murad* (intended meaning) is understood, what Allah Ta'ala’s meaning is. The meaning which is in the *zahir*, if only the *zahir* is understood, then the glorious Qur’an would be only *zahir*. But the glorious Qur’an, as you have studied in the morning lesson, has both *zahir* and *batin* (esoteric). Just as the *din* (religion) is in the *zahir* and in the *batin*. The *zahir* part was the responsibility of Mawlana Payghambar (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*), which is *tanzil* (revelation). In full detail, this has been discussed in the morning prayer. That the ranks of *tanzil* are the responsibility of our Payghambar (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*), and the glorious Qur’an was revealed to him, "hudan fi al-Qur'an" (a guidance in the Qur'an), in this very month, in the blessed month of Ramadan, the glorious Qur’an was revealed, and he explained it.

The Prophet is *Asdaq al-Qa'ilin* (the most truthful of speakers), and especially, you all know that the Prophet was called *al-Amin* (the trustworthy) and *al-Sadiq* (the truthful) from childhood. This was famous throughout all of Makkah; the polytheists of Makkah also knew that there was no one more truthful than him and no one more trustworthy than him. So in this regard, the word must be accepted.

The first part is *tanzil*. But he did not explain it, meaning its rulings, the rulings of the glorious Qur’an, like when the morning prayer should be prayed, the noon prayer, how many *rak’at*s there are, he explained all these details, but he did not explain the *ta’wil*. The responsibility of *ta’wil* belongs to the *Wasi al-Payghambar* (legatee of the Prophet), and Mawlana 'Ali (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*) is its rightful authority. So this means that the *din* has two parts: *tanzil* and *ta’wil*. *Tanzil* means to descend. The glorious Qur’an which is the book that was sent down, this is the highest book revealed by Allah Ta'ala. Remember this, that there is the *Zabur*, the *Tawrat*, the *Injil*, and the Qur’an. These four books have come from Allah, and besides these, there are also the *Suhuf* (scrolls) of Ibrahim and other scrolls have also come. But the difference in all of them is this: that according to their respective times, the *Injil* was also the best, the *Tawrat* also is, and the *Zabur* also is. But the glorious Qur’an is the *Furqan al-'Azim* (the Great Criterion), which has come from a great, high sphere, and it is called the *Furqan al-'Azim*. And Payghambar (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*), since he is *Sayyid al-Mursalin* (Master of the Messengers), he is greater than all the prophets, so whatever scrolls were revealed upon the prophets, this is greater than them. And this is the last sign of Allah Ta'ala, that the glorious Qur’an is a great miracle (*mu'jizah*) of Payghambar (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*), that what he heard, "wa ma yantiqu 'an al-hawa in huwa illa wahyun yuha" (He does not speak from [his own] inclination. It is not but a revelation revealed). The Prophet did not speak his own words until revelation came to him. He would speak the same words that were revealed.

This means that what you are hearing, you should also accept it and affirm that behind every meaning, there is another meaning. And now this is a book of *ta’wil*, and beyond this are *haqa'iq* (realities), beyond that are *daqa'iq* (subtleties), then *haqa'iq 'aliyah* (higher realities), then *daqa'iq al-daqa'iq* (subtleties of subtleties), and then *ifadat* (emanations). Knowledge has levels and it has stages, that one who studies, learns according to his capacity (*istiqaq*) and as much as he is given, as much as is his ability (*maghdur*).

Here, in this second *juz'*, in the verses that have started from the beginning, you should understand what the meaning is. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim.
Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allahumma salli 'ala Sayyidina Muhammadin wa 'ala ali Sayyidina Muhammad.
Please read a little louder.
Slowly. "La junaha 'alaykum..."
"La junaha 'alaykum in tallaqtum al-nisa'a ma lam tamassuhunna aw tafridu lahunna faridatan, wa matti'uhunna..." (There is no blame upon you if you divorce women you have not touched or for whom you have not specified a dowry. But give them a provision...)
There is no sin upon you if you divorce your wives in a state where you have not had intercourse with them and their dower (*mahr*) has not been fixed. A little louder, please.
Meaning, here the address...
Now see, this is related to wives. You all know that in religion, if there is no meaning, if there is no esoteric meaning, then religion becomes very easy. Every person becomes a scholar. So this means that wherever rulings have come, wherever rulings have come, behind them there is a meaning. That there is no sin upon you if you divorce your wives in a state where you have not had intercourse with them. Until there is a meeting, then divorce your wives in a state… divorce them, that you have not had intercourse with them. And their dower has not been fixed. Dower. You all know, it always happens in our gatherings, that a dower, a *wali* (guardian), a *qadi* (judge), and witnesses, these five items are famous for everyone, that without them, a marriage does not take place. Here, what is the meaning of wives? One is the giver, one is the receiver. Whatever women there are, they take some things from men. And women are the receivers, men are the givers. In this way, the foundation of religion has been laid, "asasa al-dinu 'ala mithal al-khalq" (religion is founded on the example of creation). The example of creation that you see in the world, according to that, things have been decided. So there is no sin, there is no harm. "La junaha 'alaykum" (no blame upon you). Divorce your wives in a state where you have not had intercourse with them, and their dower has not been fixed. Meaning, here the address is what? That the one who is teaching, who is in the position of a man. For this reason, those who are very learned within the Jama'at or in Jamia al-Sharif, they are called *rijal al-da'wah* (men of the mission). *Rijal al-da'wah* means men. They are in the position of men. So *rijal al-da'wah* is a separate category in the books of *haqa'iq*. And if seen from this perspective, then by man it is meant that he is the one who benefits (*mufid*). The one who benefits, who teaches, his status is different, and the one who seeks benefit (*mustafid*) has a different status.
Here the address is... please read.
Meaning, here the address is to the *mufidin* (teachers), that if you wish to leave your *mustafidin* (students) in a state where you have not yet taught them esoteric knowledge (*'ilm al-batin*) or have not fixed a station (*maqam*) for them, there is no sin in doing so.
Fixing a station is the same as fixing a dower. So in such a state, if the *mufid* is teaching someone and then leaves them for some reason, he can do so. "La junaha 'alaykum" (no blame upon you). There is no sin upon you for this. The *mufid* is in the position of a man, and the woman, in her position, is the *mustafid*.

*'Ilm al-batin* (esoteric knowledge). Now see, this is the blessing of Ramadan Sharif, that here we are all *mustafidin*, young and old, less intelligent, more intelligent, all are being told together... otherwise, the rules of the lesson are that one should teach as much as the student is worthy of. The elders of the past who used to teach, it's about that time, that they would see the capacity in front of them and teach the *mustafidin* only that much. So the young and old do not become one. Every person who is a *mustafid* is at the rank of a *mustajib* (respondent). A *mustajib* is the first rank of acceptance, that is, of learners. And after that, there are the ranks of a *mu'min*. You have all heard that a *mu'min* has ten ranks. Slowly, slowly, one keeps learning. And from that, the spiritual form (*surat-i ruhani*) is formed. Regarding the spiritual form, I have explained before as well, that the spiritual form is not one, but there is also a form of action (*surat-i 'amali*) and a form of knowledge (*surat-i 'ilmi*). The form of action is formed as a separate matter, that you attested to the Prophet, believed in the oneness (*tawhid*) of Allah, and after that, obeyed the *Shari'at* of the Prophet. So this, "Ashhadu an la ilaha illallah wa ashhadu anna Muhammadan rasulullah" (I bear witness that there is no god but Allah, and I bear witness that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah), to say this expresses that you have adopted the form of action. And after that, just as it is among the common people, that the first is the first *kalimah*, the second *kalimah*, the third *kalimah* in the *fiqh*, in the same way, we also do this. But after that, the opportunity to learn, slowly, slowly, as one gains awareness, and understands what needs to be understood, then the form of knowledge (*'ilmi surat*) begins to form. So the form of knowledge is separate, and the form of action is separate.

So here too, the address is to the *mufidin*. Here, meaning here, the address is to the *mufidin*, that if you leave your *mustafidin*, the ones below you, and you want to leave them in a state where you have not yet taught them *'ilm al-batin* completely. Now, whatever is taught, we think that this is all the knowledge, but it's not like that. Rather, above every scholar, there is another scholar. One who teaches, teaches what he knows. The one who has more knowledge, teaches more. So this means that *'ilm al-batin* is a hidden thing, and these, the sciences of *haqa'iq*, they all go and meet with Mawlana 'Ali (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*). No matter who teaches, it emerges from 'Ali.

So, you have not yet taught them *'ilm al-batin*. They were studying, but for some reason, the gatherings stopped, so he said, leave them. Now don't teach them. If there is an order from a higher authority that you should stop the lesson, this has happened. Here, in the time of Hazrat Is'haq Sahib, there was a Sayyid, Sayyid Husayn bin Ahmad, at that time it happened that throughout the island, Sayyid Husayn bin Ahmad used to teach here. But his accent was Arabic, Yemeni accent. Today there are many who speak Arabic, but at that time it was difficult to understand. And he used to teach, so doubts arose among the people. So it was said that all lessons should be stopped. Sayyidna Ghulam Husayn Sahib, or let's say during Sayyid bin Ahmad's time, the order came to stop all lessons except for Is'haq's. Babazat's lesson was the only one that continued in Hindustan. All lessons were stopped.
So the meaning is, so that no doubt (*ishtibah*) arises in the *din*. Here too, it's the same thing, that in the *din*, we have to remain firm in our conviction (*iqan*), that the covenant (*'ahd*) we take, the thing we are naming, are we believing in it or not? Are we just saying it from the heart, or from the tongue? I have explained this in great detail in the morning, that "tasdiqun bi al-jinan, qawlun bi al-lisan, wa 'amalun bi al-arkan" (affirmation with the heart, utterance with the tongue, and action with the limbs), these three things together make up faith (*iman*). So faith has a special quality, that when you fulfill all three, you receive faith.
Or fix their station. Fixing a station means the same dower. In simple language, what do the Muslims have? Well, if you want to leave them after marriage, then leave them, or what should they do? If they don't fix their dower, then leave them, it doesn't matter. So there is no sin in doing so.
Now what meaning will you take from this? Even in simple language, there are rulings, the Prophet's rulings, which we have to attest to. But to understand their meaning is the work of intelligent people. "Afala ta'qilun, afala tubsiruun" (Do you not reason? Do you not see?). Allah repeatedly says this, "Why don't you reflect on it?" Do a review of what is being explained within.
So the meaning is, there is no sin in doing so.
"Wa matti'uhunna 'ala al-musi'i qadaruhu wa 'ala al-muqtiri qadaruhu, mata'an bi al-ma'ruf, haqqan 'ala al-muhsinin." (But give them a provision, upon the wealthy according to his means, and upon the poor according to his means, a provision according to what is acceptable - a duty upon the doers of good.)
However, give them some gift (*bakhshish*). The wealthy according to his capacity, and the poor according to his capacity. A little louder.
According to the Shari'ah ruling. It is obligatory upon a *mu'min*. Meaning, however, do not keep the *mustafidin* completely deprived. Before leaving, increase their knowledge of the exoteric sciences (*'ilm al-zahir*) to some extent. Every person should teach according to his knowledge. A great scholar should teach according to his capacity of knowledge. He should not be deficient. And a junior scholar should teach what he knows according to his capacity of knowledge. However, he should not transgress the limits of the *zahir*.
He should not transgress the *zahir*. A junior scholar or a great scholar, each should explain according to their own limit. A great scholar, if he wants to explain, should not leave anything out, he should explain as much as he can. And the junior scholar, if he doesn't do it... to transgress the limit is wrong. The limit (*had*) that one has, *hudud* is the plural of *had*. The *hudud* of the *din*, all of them are a *had*. They are a milestone, like five miles, six miles, seven miles, the sign that is placed. So one who transgresses the limit is not within the *hudud*. So from my perspective too, it's the same thing, that to transgress the limit is wrong. That if the limit is transgressed, he is not within the *hudud*. He who transgressed the limit. So if seen from this perspective, then...
Even if we don't teach them, the *mustafidin* were studying and we left them, then give them some gift. For example, do this, that you memorize the *sahifah*, I will explain it later. Or that you keep reading the *da'a'im al-islam*, Insha'Allah when the time comes, after a year or two, I will explain. This is what happens. What does it mean? Give them some gift. The wealthy according to his capacity, and the poor according to his capacity. Wealthy here means the more knowledgeable. This is the translation of the Qur'anic verse. And the poor according to his capacity. That is, the one with less knowledge, the *mufid* who is a junior scholar, should explain according to his capacity. He shouldn't explain big things. A junior *mufid* is explaining big things, that's not right. Whatever authority you have been given, it is better if you explain more of that. Because the most important thing in religion is that one should teach according to the capacity of the one in front, and besides that, the thing that gets imprinted in the mind is what matters. It's not that it should go over one's head. It came up in the morning lesson as well, and you heard in the gathering yesterday, that Mawlana Hasan (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*), who was the elder grandson, he said that what one sees with the eyes is the truth, and what is heard with the ears is falsehood (*batil*). It's a lie. So between the ear and the eye, there is a distance of four fingers. This, we think, is a great saying from here, but this is the saying of Mawlana Hasan (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*), that there is a distance of four fingers between the eye and the ear. But what comes out of the ear is not true. Hearsay, which is common among us, the information we gather about the world, is what is it? Audio-visual is a different thing, to see with the eyes and hear with the ears. But what is only heard with the ears, that thing is not true, it is falsehood. And in the morning you have also studied that by *mu'min* it is meant a "thing" (*shay'*). "Wa kulla shay'in ahsaynahu fi imamin mubin" (And everything We have accounted for in a clear Imam). We have placed everything in the *Imam Mubin*. We have placed everything in the *Imam Mubin*. Now if you reflect on this, it will become clear. See, I am explaining a little to make you understand. That "wa kulla shay'in ahsaynahu fi imamin mubin." About 25-30 years ago, this thought came to my mind, and at that time our elder, Mulla Abbas Sahib, was in Bombay. There was a lot of rain, and at that time I had this thought and I told Mulla Abbas Sahib, and he said it was correct. That is, that "wa kulla shay'in ahsaynahu fi imamin mubin". Every thing, we have placed in the *Imam Mubin*. Now if you reflect, in the *zahir*, everything is not in the *Imam Mubin*. There is the *Kitab Mubin* (clear book) and the *Imam Mubin*. As it comes in the verse. So by book and Imam, the same thing is meant. So everything is not apparently visible in the *Imam Mubin*, so it must have some meaning. And the meaning is this. See, if you analyze each thing, just as we do in English, we analyze, this is a verb, this is a subject, this is... In that way, if you see, then "wa kulla shay'in ahsaynahu". What is *kulla shay'* (every thing)? What is a thing? So regarding a thing, it has been said that a thing is the rank of a *mu'min*. Every *mu'min*. "Wa kulla shay'in ahsaynahu". *Ahsaynahu* is what they call statistics, which is arranged in a numerical order, that is called statistics. And Allah Ta'ala could have said "wa ja'alna kulla shay'in fi imamin mubin" (And We placed everything in the *Imam Mubin*). The word "ja'alna" could have come, the word "sana'a" could have come. "We have gathered everything... in the *Imam Mubin*." These are four or five words, but "ahsaynahu" has come. So I reflected on that and I have explained it there. At that time, it was Hazrat Zayni Sahib's, Nasrullah Sahib's time, maybe. It's about a long time ago, 35-40 years ago. That why did "wa kulla shay'in ahsaynahu" come? So I thought that "ahsaynahu" means to join in an orderly fashion. If something is gathered, as Sayyidna Ghulam Raza says, if 50 people from different sects gather in one place, they are a crowd, a mob, a group, not a *jama'at* (congregation). A *jama'at* is one where people of one thought, one religion, one belief, even if there are two people, it is a *jama'at*. This is what Sayyidna Ghulam Raza Sahib said. So from this perspective, the word "jama'na" (we gathered) is not correct. Only "ahsaynahu" is. *Ahsaynahu* means we joined in an orderly fashion. Then I reflected further, that *Imam Mubin*, why was *Imam* said and not just *Mubin*? Why did the word *Mubin* come? *Mubin* means manifest, visible, that is apparent, visible in front. So now, the manifest Imams, in them we have joined in an orderly fashion. What thing has been joined? So one is the rank of Imam, you have all read the *mabda'* and *ma'ad*, you know that there is a *Lahut* (divine aspect) and a *Nasut* (human aspect). *Nasut* is called the body of the Imam, his blessed body, in which many things are gathered. And in the *Lahut* also things are gathered. So what is gathered in the *Lahut*? *Suwarat* (spiritual forms). In the *Lahut*, in the *haykal* (celestial body) of the Imam, the *Suwarat* are gathered, and in the *Nasut*, the *rihiyat* (spiritual essences) are gathered. So the *rihiyat* are of a lower rank than the *suwarat*. And that is why the *rihiyat* sometimes appear to the *mukhlisin* (sincere ones), that is why Imam Husayn also appeared, Imam Hasan also appeared... But the *haykal* is not seen. The *suwarat* are not seen. That is why the *suwarat* are there. So "wa kulla shay'in ahsaynahu", we have... in the *Imam Mubin*, that Imam who is visible, in him we have joined the *rihiyat* in an orderly fashion. "Wa kulla shay'in ahsaynahu". Now see, *shay'*... we have studied in the morning, that by *shay'* (thing) it is meant the *mu'min*, the true *mu'min*, the perfect *mu'min*, he is called a *mu'min*. The second thing that is there is *nisf shay'* (half a thing). In the book that was read yesterday, it was written there that what is *nisf shay'*? *Nisf shay'* is the hypocrite (*munafiq*). He is a *mu'min*, he has taken the covenant, but he is a hypocrite. The hypocrite is half a thing. Half a thing. I will tell you its meaning. And *la shay'* (no thing) is the infidel (*kafir*). He says in the *du'a*, that *la shay'*. Just as we understand, if the soul leaves a person, he is a corpse (*lashah*). *Lashah* is *la shay'*. The corpse is called *lashah*. And it is when the soul leaves. So in this way... Now see, hypocrite. What is the definition of a hypocrite? You can look it up in the lesson of Chan, from Sayyidna Ghulam Raza Sahib, it's a very scholarly discussion, and in it, it has been explained what the signs of a hypocrite are, etc. You have all read it. But the point is, who is a hypocrite? A hypocrite has one thing in his heart and says another thing with his tongue. He is a hypocrite. But what is its literal meaning? It comes from *nafaq*. *Nafaq* is what they call a tunnel, a burrow. The burrow is one that enters from one side and exits from the other. And for this reason, Mawlana Hasan (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*) said that what is heard by the ear is falsehood. It enters one ear and exits the other. So *nafaq*, hypocrite is on its measure. And *la shay'* is the infidel, who does not believe in Allah and does not believe in His rulings. So in this way, here too, the point has come... that is... what is it? That everyone should be explained according to his capacity, he should not transgress. However, he should not transgress the limits of the *zahir*. He should take care of the rank of the *mufid*, of the *mustafid*. This is the way of the *mu'min*. A little louder. "Wa in tallaqtumuhunna min qabli an tamassuhunna wa qad faradtum lahunna faridatan fa nisfu ma faradtum." (And if you divorce them before you have touched them and you have already specified for them a dowry, then [give] half of what you specified). And if you have divorced them before intercourse, and you have fixed a dower, then give half the dower. Yes. Meaning, if you have not yet taught the *mustafidin* *'ilm al-batin*, but you had made it incumbent upon yourselves to teach certain lessons, then you must fulfill that. If not the whole, then at least half of it. But if he has forgiven you... and doesn't insist... then it is another matter. "Illa an ya'funa aw ya'fuwa alladhi bi yadihi 'uqdat al-nikah." (Unless they [the women] forgo it or he in whose hand is the marriage contract forgoes it). Except that she forgives, or the person in whose hand is the knot of the marriage. Yes, husband or guardian. Husband or guardian. Meaning, by *'uqdat al-nikah* it is meant... That is, either the *mustafid* does not insist on his right and leaves it, or the one who is their *hadd-i 'ali* (higher authority), who has joined them both, makes a compromise between them. Meaning, the order of the higher authority. As I have just explained, that if a higher authority says, "Alright, you have to take it," then take it, it's fine. Or if they say, "Leave it," then leave it. As is the order. They should compromise between themselves and get him to forgive. "Wa an ta'fu aqrabu li al-taqwa, wa la tansawu al-fadla baynakum. Inna Allaha bima ta'maluna basir." (And to forgo is nearer to righteousness. And do not forget graciousness between you. Indeed, Allah is Seeing of what you do.) And if you forgive, then it is closer to piety. And do not forget to do good to each other. And your deeds... And Allah is watching your deeds. Meaning, to forgive, is to save oneself from opposing the Imam. And remember, do not forget to do good. See, here it is, that is, to forgive, is to save oneself from opposing the Imam. It should not be that you deal with people in such a way, that they turn away even from the Imam. The one with faith, his faith gets corrupted. You shouldn't do that, but you should explain to them and bring them around. Saving oneself from opposing the Imam is more helpful. And remember, do not forget to do good. See, wherever... the mention of the Imam comes, there in the Qur'an Sharif, the name of Allah comes. "Inna Allaha bima ta'maluna basir." Allah is watching all your deeds. The Imam is watching all your deeds. This means that the Imam is watching. Now this is not among the common people, this is called *ta'wil*. That what we mean is that this Sayyid Husayn Husamuddin wrote this. And Sayyid Husayn Husamuddin is the one who prayed at a grave. Where? He went to a shrine, and it was before Sayyidna Hibatullah, during Sayyidna Ibrahim's time. Sayyidna Ibrahim (*Quddusullahu ruhu*), and with him was Sayyidna Husayn Husamuddin, and there was a man from Multan. And at that time, the Da'i had this thought, an idea came to him, that at this time the *ishaqat* (emanations) of the Imam are occurring, whatever prayer is made will be accepted. Quickly, do it quickly, whatever you have to do. So immediately, the prayer was made. So now, who prayed for what? That Multani Sahib did not understand what to pray for. He said, "May my handwriting become good." His handwriting became very good. Sayyid Husayn Husamuddin, who was with him, a great scholar, he said, "May Allah Ta'ala grant me such knowledge that no one else has." This was his prayer. And so it happened, that the *ta'wil* of the glorious Qur’an, which we have, was written 250 years ago. It is famous among the common people that they don't have a *tafsir* (exegesis). The *tafsirs* are filled in the world. And in the *tafsirs*, they have split hairs, why this *zabar* (vowel mark), why this *zer*, why this *pesh*. But they have missed the main point. So this means that they remained stuck in *zabar*, *zer*, *pesh*. And they got divided over pronouns (*zamir*). The pronoun *ha* goes here, the pronoun *he* goes... अरे, understand the main point, the gist. What's the use of getting stuck in pronouns? One whose own conscience (*zamir*) is not right, he looks for pronouns. So this means that the common people, they all... in the nuances... you can read the *tafsir*. There are great *tafsirs*. Why this came, why that came. For understanding, it is good, in a general sense. But where we have to study our sciences, you have to sit with your head bowed, sitting on your knees, then the knowledge of 'Ali comes. So this means that there are many *tafsirs*, but there is no *ta'wil*. "Wa ma ya'lamu ta'wilahu illa Allah wa al-rasikhuna fi al-'ilm" (And no one knows its interpretation except Allah and those firm in knowledge). And no one knows the *ta'wil* except Allah and those who are firm in knowledge. These people say, "Only Allah knows." The Mian Sahabs know, and no one else knows what it means. It's not in my control. Ask someone else. "Fas'alu ahl al-dhikr in kuntum la ta'lamun" (So ask the people of remembrance if you do not know). If you don't know, then ask a person of remembrance. They don't want to ask that. So they don't believe in *ta'wil*. Whereas "wa al-rasikhuna fi al-'ilm" (and those firm in knowledge) are our Imams (*Salawatullah 'Alayhim*), who are *rasikh*, firmly rooted in knowledge. And those who have gleaned from them are the *Du'at*. And below the *Du'at*, and below them. It has been coming down. So this means that knowledge is obtained from above. From below, knowledge never goes up. From the low ground to the high ground it will not go, from below to above it will not go. From above to below. That is why when it rains, it rains from the sky, it doesn't come from the earth. It rains from above. "Anzalna min al-ma'i... min al-sama'i ila al-ard" (We sent down water from the sky to the earth). From the sky to the earth. Now see, there are interpretations in this. I am only here for a few days, but as much as I can explain, is that by sky here it is not meant this sky. The whole world knows that it rains from the sky. But what is its real meaning? The speaking *natiq* of the time, the person of the Prophet (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*), he is meant by the sky. Always, wherever you find the mention of "al-sama' wa al-ard" (the sky and the earth) in our glorious Qur’an, remember there that by sky is meant the Prophet, and by earth is meant 'Ali (*Salawatullah 'Alayh*). When the water rains, it will come to the earth. It won't go anywhere else. But if you stop it by force, then it will stop somewhere. By force, it happens. Forcibly. Otherwise, in the same way, the knowledge that the Prophet had, was all given to 'Ali. From 'Ali we have acquired it. Not from the Prophet. The Prophet only had this nobility, that the glorious Qur’an came to his blessed heart. The light of the Qur’an. The Qur’an Sharif did not come. The light of the Qur’an came. In the form of light. This is the greatness of the Prophet. The light of the Qur’an... I won't go into details now, that your heart was washed, and that happened, and this happened, then revelation came to you and it began. So the Qur’an in the heart... that is... the Qur’an descended upon your heart in the form of light. And what you explained, became the Qur’an. So this means that the Prophet was truthful and trustworthy, that is why we believed. And this is the highest book, and we are studying it. "Hafizu 'ala al-salawat..." Now see, the arrangement of the glorious Qur’an, or let's say its... is that now the matter of marriage, its mention was going on, and now a second... topic has started. This is the special miracle (*i'jaz*) of the glorious Qur’an, that every sentence may be different, but it is complete in its own place. What is it? "Hafizu 'ala al-salawat wa al-salat al-wusta" (Guard the prayers, and [especially] the middle prayer). Be punctual in your prayers, especially the middle prayer. Meaning, the esoteric, spiritual, and gnostic assemblies, be regular in attending them. Do not be neglectful. And especially the *wasi* (legatee) and the *'alim al-zahir* (exoteric scholar) who is intermediate between the *natiq* (speaker) and the *wasi*, never miss his assembly. Yes. This, the *Salat al-Wusta* (middle prayer) is the noon prayer. From one perspective, from another perspective it is the Fajr prayer. And here, Mulla Amir Sahib has written both, the Friday prayer. But the noon prayer, because the noon prayer is the first one that was prayed in Islam. When the Prophet brought Islam, the *Shari'at* of the Prophet, at that time the first prayer that was prayed was the noon prayer. And during noon, at the time of noon, just as the sun is at its zenith and then starts to decline a little, in the same way, when the Prophet was 40 years old, revelation came to him and he began to explain. So this means that noon, *Salat al-Wusta*. You should be punctual in your prayers and *Salat al-Wusta*. Meaning, the noon prayer, which is the middle prayer, that is, between Zuhr and 'Asr, you should pray it. "Wa qumu lillahi qanitin." (And stand before Allah in a devout manner). And stand before Allah with devotion and keep praying. Meaning, stand firm in the *da'wah* (mission) of the *Wasi* and keep inviting. "Fa in khiftum fa rijalan aw rukbanan." (And if you fear, then [pray] on foot or riding). Then if there is fear, then pray while walking or on a ride. Meaning, if you have to practice *taqiyyah* due to the fear of enemies in spreading the *da'wah* of the *Wasi*, then preach secretly while walking, among the believers. And *rukbanan*. *Taqiyyah*. *Taqiyyah* is what Mawlana Ja'far al-Sadiq says, that *taqiyyah* is my religion and the religion of my forefathers. That where the situation is bad... the situation in religion does not always remain the same. The situations that occurred after the Prophet in Islam, in them, a lot of *taqiyyah* had to be practiced. During the time of Ja'far al-Sadiq and after him, complete silence was observed. So *taqiyyah* is this, that we are praying in the prayer of the common people, going to their mosque and praying, then we pray with our hands tied. There is no harm. We know the meaning, that we should leave our hands, but we don't have to cause a commotion. We don't have to create quarrels in Islam. So we tie our hands. Tying the hands and releasing the hands, what does that mean? You all know the meaning, you have been studying it for years. So it is that hands are tied in this way, that the Prophet first prayed with his hands tied. But once he left it. So is the Prophet's second action better or the first action? When he left it, then we should adopt the second action. What does that mean? That he used to tie his hands like this. Now among the Sunnis, there is this thing, to tie it here, to tie it here, there are different ways. The Malikis are different, the Hanafis are different. But those who tie them, we place them on the chest. So the chest is the center of secrets, of keeping something hidden. As long as the Prophet kept the station of his *Wasi* hidden, he used to pray with his hands tied. As soon as the Prophet announced, the verse "balligh ma unzila ilayka" (proclaim what has been revealed to you) was revealed, he announced at Ghadir Khumm, he left it. Now these people don't accept it. They say, "No, the Prophet used to pray with his hands tied." So if we go and pray in the mosque of others and tie our hands, there is no harm. But we know what it means. Go on. And *rukbanan*, meaning the lower *hudud* who are under your authority, benefit them with esoteric and gnostic knowledge. "Fa idha amintum fa-dhkuru Allaha kama 'allamakum ma lam takunu ta'lamun." (But when you are safe, then remember Allah as He has taught you that which you did not know). So when the fear is gone, then remember Allah, just as He has taught you what you did not know. Meaning, when the fear of the enemies is gone, then spread the *da'wah* of the *Wasi* openly. Just as he has taught you the method of *da'wah*. Arrange it in the same way, for which method you were previously unaware. Therefore, act according to the method he has taught you. "Wa alladhina yutawaffawna minkum wa yadharuna azwajan wasiyyatan li azwajihim mata'an ila al-hawli ghayra ikhraj." (And those who are taken in death among you and leave wives behind - a bequest for their wives is a provision for a year without turning [them] out). And those among you who are dying and leaving wives behind, they should make a will for their wives that for a year, they should be given the expense of food and drink, and not be turned out of the house. Meaning, if an elder of the *da'wah* passes away, and in his *da'wah*, his *muzawij* (spiritual counterparts) are there, who are in the position of wives, then they should be allowed to remain in their station. They should not be removed. As long as the *da'wah* of the Imam of the time remains in that area where the elder of the *da'wah* was appointed for service. These are all matters of the *din*, just as we understand the rulings of marriage, that the wives should be given maintenance, if they are divorced, they should be given money. Behind all of this, there is a meaning. "Fa in kharajna fa la junaha 'alaykum fima fa'alna fi anfusihinna min ma'ruf. Wa Allahu 'azizun hakim." (But if they leave, there is no blame upon you for what they do with themselves in an acceptable way. And Allah is Exalted in Might, Wise). And if they leave on their own, then there is no sin upon you in what they have done for themselves according to the Shari'ah. And Allah is dominant, wise. Meaning, if the surviving students (*pas-mandah azwaj*) of the deceased elder want to connect with another elder of the *da'wah* and prefer to leave their own station, then they have the authority to do so. They can organize themselves wherever and with whomever they want, remaining within the *hudud* of the *din*. And there will be no blame on those in higher authority. That's enough. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Ya Allah, salli 'ala Sayyidina Muhammad wa ali Sayyidina Muhammad. Allahumma salli 'ala Sayyidina Muhammad wa ali Sayyidina Muhammad wa ali Sayyidina Muhammad. Salli 'ala jami' al-anbiya' wa al-mursalin. Al-Fatihah. Two rak'at? Two rak'at. Do it quickly. Two rak'at of qada' namaz, make the intention. For qada' namaz of 'Isha, two rak'at, make the intention. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allahumma inni nawaytu an usalliya salata rak'atayn qada'in li salat al-'Isha' lillahi ta'ala wa 'l-Imam, mustaqbilan ila jihati al-ka'bah al-sharifah al-harami, Allahu Akbar. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allahumma salli 'ala Sayyidina Muhammadin wa 'ala ali Sayyidina Muhammad wa li-waliyih. Ya Karimu ya Karimu, ya Qayyumu, ya 'Alimu ya 'Alimu ya 'Alimu, ya 'Alimu kulla shay'in, 'allimni shay'an ya 'Alim.

<!-- AUG:ibrahim-2015 lecture 2 -->

Three pages were done, right? Sir... Umm... on the second page... finally... meaning three... Page number? Starts from four... It was done on three... three was done. ... Let's start from the beginning... Read... *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allahumma salli 'ala Sayyidina Muhammadin wa 'ala aalihi wa sallim* (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. O Allah, bless our master Muhammad and his family and grant them peace). *Wa alladhina...* Make the fans slower... make the fans slower... The fan behind... make that fan slower. If there's no one there, then turn it off. ... If there's no one, then turn it off, brother. Because the fans are making noise. Yes, go ahead.

*Wa alladhina yutawaffawna minkum wa yadharuna azwajan wasiyatan li azwajihim mata'an ila al-hawli ghayra ikhraj* (And those of you who are taken in death and leave wives should bequeath for their wives a year's maintenance and residence without turning them out). ... And those of you who... No, what's the number? ... Three, three... And those among you who are passing away and leave behind wives, they should make a will for their wives, that for a year's time, they should be given maintenance and not be expelled from the house. Meaning, if a senior *da'i* passes away and his followers are in his wife's quarters, then they should be allowed to remain in their place. ...should be allowed to... not be removed, until the Imam of the time, in the *da'wah* residence where that senior *da'i* was established for service, remains. ...in his... wife's... in the wife's quarters, the junior *da'i* should not be moved from his place and quarters.

Yes. Yesterday's discourse is continuing, in which it was stated that there is a rank of the *Mufid* and the *Mustafid*, and it was explained that if a senior *da'i* passes away, then his followers, who are in his wife's quarters or are his subordinates, should not be removed from their place, and should be kept established.

*Fa in kharajna... fa in kharajna fala junaha 'alaykum fi ma fa'alna fi anfusihinna min ma'rufin wallahu 'azizun hakim* (But if they leave, there is no blame on you for what they do with themselves in an acceptable way. And Allah is Exalted in Might, Wise). And if they leave on their own, then there is no sin upon you in what they have done for themselves according to the ruling of the *shari'ah*. And Allah is all-powerful, all-wise. Meaning, if the survivors of the deceased wish to be utilized in the service of another senior *da'i* and willingly decide to leave their place, then they have that authority. Wherever and with whomever they wish, they can settle within the bounds of the *deen*. No blame will be placed upon those in higher ranks. Imam *'alayhi al-salam*... These are all rulings that are to be understood, that if a senior *da'i* passes away, then after that, the one who is remaining can go to another and can be utilized. Meaning, the surviving followers. There is no sin in it, *la junaha*. The verse that started yesterday, from yesterday, it is *la junaha*, that there is no sin in it, no cause for censure. Wherever and with whomever they wish, they can become organized within the bounds of the *deen*. Within the bounds of the *deen*.

Now look, this is a bit... these are the final verses of *Surah al-Nisa'* and *al-Baqarah*, in which there is the matter of marriage and all these things. We haven't found the first book. I looked for it today too... it couldn't be found. Because in it, there are explanations that everyone can understand. This... there are many things in it that are related to inheritance, meaning ancestral property, or related to marriage, related to divorce. It is a bit difficult for us to understand. So remember that if the first book is found, then tomorrow, *insha'Allah*, we will read it. That is much better. These are all verses of the Holy Quran, but in some places it is difficult, in some places it is easy to understand.

*Wa lil mutallaqat... Wa lil mutallaqati mata'un bil ma'rufi haqqan 'ala al-muttaqin* (And for divorced women is a provision according to what is acceptable - a duty upon the righteous). And for divorced women, to provide them benefit according to the established custom is an obligatory right upon the God-fearing. Yes. It is an obligatory right. Meaning, if a *Mufid* separates his *Mustafid* from himself, it is still incumbent upon him not to deprive them of the externals of the *shari'ah*. Yes. If... if, suppose, the *Mufid* leaves... stops... stops teaching, as happens, then even at that time, it is necessary to adhere to the external commands. Keep it to the level of *tanzil* (exoteric). If, suppose, one doesn't have the aptitude for *ta'wil*, then it is necessary to teach up to the level of *tanzil*. Give the knowledge of the *zahir* (external) for sure. They should not be deprived of the *shari'ah*. In gatherings... Look... in gatherings and for admonition, keep him included. Through gatherings... The thing is, not everyone comes to the lessons, so a high-level matter is not explained. In gatherings, with the rulings of the *shari'ah*, he should make it manifest. The believing *mustafid*... it is an obligatory right upon him, he should take care of it. Yes.

*Kadhalika yubayyinu Allahu lakum ayatihi la'allakum ta'qilun* (Thus does Allah make clear to you His verses that you might use reason). In this way, Allah explains His signs to you so that you may understand. Here, Mawlana Imam, the laws of... Look, 'signs' (*ayat*) refers to signs. Allah is explaining His signs to you. *Afala...* What is it? *la'allakum ta'qilun*. So that you may use reason. Do you not have intellect? Do you not develop understanding? So that you may understand what mystery is in Allah's signs. So Allah's signs, some are clear (*muhkamat*), some are allegorical (*mutashabihat*), some are decisive (*muhkam*). The verses have different aspects. So in it, some things are explained openly, some things are not. And what has not been explained, our *du'at* have expressed it, how it has meaning. There is the explanation of Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq, and that of Mawlana Baqir, these are the explanations. Otherwise, the world... everyone is clinging to the *zahir* (external). Whatever external rulings there are, just follow them. This was said, that's it. Although there are many things in it that are beyond their understanding. Meaning, Mawlana Imam, the laws of *deen*... Look, where the word 'Allah' has come, there Imam will come. And where the discussion starts with 'meaning', that is the *ta'wil*. The rest is the translation. Meaning, Mawlana Imam explains the laws of *deen* to you so that its purposes may become clear to you. Yes.

*Alam tara ila alladhina...* Read the verses a bit... slowly... so that it can be heard... Yesterday the sound was good. If you could be a bit louder, that would be good. The verses. Read the verses a bit loudly. *Alam tara ila alladhina kharaju min diyarihim wa hum ulufun hadhara al-mawti fa qala lahum Allahu mutu thumma ahyahum* (Have you not considered those who left their homes in many thousands, fearing death? Allah said to them, "Die"; then He restored them to life). Have you not seen those people who left their homes in fear of death, and they were in the thousands? Then Allah said to them, 'Die,' then He brought them back to life. Meaning, *mutu wa ahyakum*. These people who die... what it means is the death of ignorance. They died the death of ignorance, and after that, we will... resurrect them. And this... this thing shows that those who die are also brought back to life. Resurrection is a reality (*ba'thun haqq*). The common people think that life has been given, and in this 50-60 year life, do whatever you want. It's not like that. Rather, the system of cycles (*karrati nizam*), which is the system of the *da'wah* from our side and has been explained by the Imams, peace be upon them, is that it's not just once. You will have to be reborn thousands of times. Thousands of times you will have to come into the system of cycles. Then the matter will be complete. Because the impurity we brought from there while falling is so great. What we see today, as 5 feet or 6 feet tall, we are not just this. You are much more. What you eat daily, you have eaten thousands of maunds. Where did it go? It is all with you. There is a channel, of the human constitution, that a thing comes and it goes. So which thing comes and which thing goes? The thing that leaves is separate, and the thing that comes is separate. So when it comes, are you eating it considering it licit (*halal*) or illicit (*haram*)? If you take an illicit thing, there will be corruption in your stomach. ... So the point is that things are divided into different categories. Until the final statement, which is that you should eat everything by saying *Bismillah*. So in it... It has been explained by the *du'at* that the chemistry inside, the thing, its very nature changes. If *Bismillah* is correct... I mean, just by reciting *Bismillah*, nothing happens. If one studies the letters of *Bismillah* and its meaning and then recites it, then the thing changes.

But in *halal* and *haram*, there are some things... Now look, it's the occasion of the month of Ramadan. Here and there, there are *iftar* parties, and so on, all these things you know what they are. In this, the thing that has been told is that *halal* and *haram*... this *deen* is divided. That a licit thing... use licit things, do not use illicit things. Now, sometimes it happens that illicit things are found... in licit things. And when are they found? In the blessed month of Ramadan. In the month of Ramadan, it has been told... why the bodies are kept clean. Like *taharah* (purity), which is the purity of the heart, the purity of your mind, the purity of the soul... similarly, the fast is a separate thing for the physical. The fast that we keep is to... finish our physical impurity. So in this, the venerable *du'at* say that the first thing is, why do we keep 30 fasts? What is the purpose of keeping 30 fasts? The 30 days are the 30 years that Mawlana 'Ali, peace be upon him, was silent. During the time of the caliphate, after the passing of the Prophet, for 30 years, he was silent. Those 30 days allude to that. This is the first, superficial level. The first thing, beyond that, is the rulings. That for this many... *'iddah* (waiting period), you know, the counting of days, you should complete it. How many days? 30. It cannot be one less than 30, nor one more than 30. So remember, 30 is composed of three decades. Three decades are of ten each. Now, the common people don't have three decades. Sometimes it becomes less, sometimes more. So in the three decades, it has been said that one is for forgiveness (*maghfirah*), one is for blessing (*barakah*), the three have separate aspects. So the point is that having three decades is necessary. And these three decades indicate... its meanings and realities are many more... but for us to understand, the point is that rulings for 30 days have been given that we should keep our mouths shut from eating, drinking, and intercourse. When? During the daytime. When? From when the time for *sehri* (pre-dawn meal) starts... when the time for *sehri* ends, your fast begins. And before the *Maghrib* prayer, it should end. This calculation is what has been ordained by Allah. In between this, if you have eaten or drunk anything without an excuse... if you are sick, or have a valid reason, that's a different matter... but otherwise, you have consumed something illicit. And the second thing is that the second reason... is that the 30 years are those when Mawlana 'Ali was silent. And you are... during the time of the caliphate, he was silent. So much so that it is famous among the common people that he gave the oath of allegiance (*bay'ah*) to Abu Bakr, he took it for 'Umar, for him too... there was no issue, all four caliphs were together, all are great... This is just a deception (*tawwatupi*) and nothing else. Whereas, when the caliphate of Janab Abu Bakr started, even then people said, and especially 'Umar, he said, 'Everyone is giving it, you give it too. Give your hand. Give the oath of allegiance to the first caliph.' He said, 'Alright.' Because there was compulsion. At that time, it was an era of decline, an era of corruption. It was the final era of the period of concealment (*dawr al-satr*). So what did he do? He said, 'Alright.' He extended his left hand. Now what is the meaning of extending the left hand? What is 'Ali's left hand? First, one must understand. It is a very deep matter. They said, 'You give your hand with the right hand... a promise is made, a greeting is made, everything is done with the right hand, you have given your left hand.' He said, 'I am helpless. The right hand has been given to the Prophet.' There can be no greater answer than this. So the 30 years that Janab 'Ali was silent are our 30 fasts.

And beyond that, in the language of realities (*haqa'iq*), if it is said, it is that in this month, only in this month, from when the first date starts until the last date, i.e., the *iftar* of Eid al-Fitr, until that time, what happens is that illicit things are mixed into licit things. And Allah wants to see whether you keep the fast during the day or not. So this is among the rulings of fasting, that the fast... it is a special thing. Fasts were there in the time of Musa, in the time of Janab Maryam... there were fasts. Fasts have been in every era, prayer has been too, but the methods are different. The *shari'ahs* are different. The *deen* is one. Allah's *deen* from Adam until here is one *deen*. But the *shari'ahs* keep changing. As the requirement of the time changes, the *shari'ahs* change. And the *shari'ah al-gharra'*, the Muhammadan constitution that we have, what he explained, peace be upon him, is the exact requirement of this era. Whether it is the era of the Prophet or today's era. He knew everything, what kind of era would come in the future, what would happen, and according to that, the rulings have been ordained. So *Da'a'im al-Islam*, which is our basic book, if you read it, you will realize that behind the rulings that have been given, there is a meaning. Nothing is without meaning.

So now, what is it? Death to you... that you should die. Now, from Allah's side it was said, what's the matter? *Mutu* (die) means die the death of ignorance. But then you have to be resurrected. You will have to be brought back into the world. In that... in the system of cycles, it is like this... did you not see these people... read.

Meaning, O Messenger, thousands of individuals, fearing the hardships of the test, fled from the regions of their *da'wah*. The *Wasi* stopped them from doing *da'wah* and deprived them of his spiritual effulgence (*fuyoodhat*) as well. Then he again gave them permission for *da'wah* and also issued his spiritual effulgence. Yes.

*Inna Allaha la dhu fadlin 'ala al-nasi wa lakinna akthara al-nasi la yashkurun* (Indeed, Allah is full of bounty to the people, but most of the people are not grateful). Verily, Allah is bountiful to the people, but most people do not show gratitude. Meaning, the *Wasi* bestows upon the people of the *da'wah* from his grace and generosity, but most people of the *da'wah* who do not understand these great blessings of the Lord of the *Da'wah*, do not show gratitude. Meaning, for us, this is the thing that every moment, every breath that comes and goes, we should be grateful that we are alive. If the breath stops, our actions are finished. The realm of action is that we should act as much as possible so that in the hereafter we may attain the great Paradise. What is Paradise? To be included in the *Lahut*, to be included in the *Nasut*... it is a collection of *Riyayat* and *Suwarat*. All these things, you have already read. The point is that he deprives of the intellectual effulgence (*fuyoodhat-e-'ilmi*) and then he opens the intellectual effulgence. Fuse. Fuse. Intellectual effulgence (*fuyoodhat-e-'ilmi*). *Fayz* (grace/emanation). It comes from *fayz*. Now, look... I am just giving an example... that intellectual effulgence, i.e., what does *fayz* mean? Sometimes it happens that the light doesn't turn on. Hey, you tell the child to go and see... the bulb isn't working, what's the problem? He looks... he says everything is fine, the wire inside is fine, but it's not lighting up. I don't know what the matter is. So the father will say, 'Go, look at the meter. What's wrong there?' He looks there and the fuse is loose. So the fuse, when it becomes loose, or suppose there is no connection in it... Now, with us, here, the exoteric... what I am telling about exoteric and esoteric is that the metaphorical guides (*murshidan-e-majazi*), who are seen around... there is a big difference between them and the servants of the *Da'wah*. If you see, they have worn big belts, have become great guides, with three or four people following them, and so on. But what is it? The fuse is blown. When there is no fuse, just as the light doesn't work, similarly the Divine Light (*nur-e-ilahi*) which should settle in the heart... meaning, there is no *wilayah* (love/authority) of the Commander of the Faithful. You are observing all-night vigils, standing all night long. There are *tarawih* prayers, and so on. What is *tarawih*? It is an innovation of 'Umar. It wasn't there before, in the time of the Prophet. It started in the time of Janab 'Umar. That 'I should also do something new, I am also a big person.' So this *tarawih* is his invention. More than this, there is no attention to the action. There is attention to good action.

*Ahsanu 'amala*. *Tabaraka alladhi biyadihi al-mulk... li yabluwakum ayyukum ahsanu 'amala* (Blessed is He in Whose hand is the dominion... that He may test you [as to] which of you is best in deed). So that Allah may test you, who among you does good deeds. It's not about more deeds. That you are doing *tasbih*, standing there to show people. Such people who pray to show off, they do not turn their faces towards the prayer niche, but rather turn backwards, 'Look, I am praying.' This is hypocrisy (*riya'*). It is deceit (*makr*), fraud (*fareb*). From whom? Not from the people here. The people here... The One Above is watching. What is in whose heart? Sincere prayer is a different matter. Sincere prayer... and for this reason, sincerity (*ikhlas*)... the Prophet, peace be upon him, when he started Islam, what did he say? *Qulu la ilaha illa Allah tuflihu* (Say there is no god but Allah, and you will be successful). O people, you say *la ilaha illa Allah*, you will attain salvation, you will attain success. This is the initial statement, of early Islam. And after that, *Man qala la ilaha illa Allah dakhala al-jannah* (Whoever says there is no god but Allah will enter Paradise). Whoever recites *la ilaha illa Allah*... reciting *la ilaha illa Allah* is not like that. You hear from our revered ones, how many letters are in *la ilaha illa Allah*, what jewels are there, what things are there, all that has to be understood. When you recite it then, then your *la ilaha illa Allah* is complete. And negation (*nafi*) is first, then affirmation (*ithbat*). Affirmation comes later. *La ilaha...* If you do not negate something, you cannot affirm anything. First, absolve yourself from all the gods of the world, then do it. We are also like that. If we are to believe in 'Ali, then the other caliphs who were made, you must also absolve yourself of them. It's not that we believe in 'Ali and we believe in them too. 'They are great.' As *taqiyyah* (dissimulation), it's a different matter. It has been explained that as *taqiyyah*, everything is permissible, as Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq says. But the clear matter is that if we believe in 'Ali, 'Ali is seated in our hearts. And we do not believe in others. Why do we not believe? Because they did not follow the Prophet. It's not that we don't believe in 'Ali, so 'what should we do? We don't believe, we don't believe.' But they did not believe the Prophet's word. Because *man kuntu mawlahu fa hadha 'Aliyun mawlahu* (Of whomsoever I am the master, 'Ali is his master). Janab Prophet said that 'Of whomsoever I was the master until today... "master" (*mawla*) means... it has many meanings, *mawla*. If you take it as 'master', then 'Of whomsoever I was the master, from today 'Ali is his master.' Not only this, there was a gathering of 70,000 at Ghadeer-e-Khumm. And at that time he said that... not that just 70,000 people were listening. There were countless souls there who were listening. 'Of whomsoever I am the master, from today 'Ali is his master.' So the point is that 'Ali's rank is a special rank.

And to raise a *Wasi* (successor) is a special function of the Prophet. That this... to create one's own caliph, this is necessary. Musa created Harun. He raised him. So the rank of the *Wasi* is special, because the Imamate continues from him. The spring of guidance flows from the *mustaqarr* Imams. The prophets who come in between... as I explained sometime this morning, that the *naatiqs* had a large circle, a large gathering there, in the primordial world (*'alam al-azal*). That is why it became the era of 'Isa, the era of Musa. Nowhere is the Imam's name mentioned... that it is the era of Isma'il or so-and-so... no. Rather, all eras are divided according to the *nutuqa*. So remember, the rank of the *Wasa'it*... the rank of the *Wasi* is the most important. We who have spent three months... *Rajab al-murajjab* is entirely upon Janab 'Ali, peace be upon him. You are the seventh *wasi*, and it is the seventh month among the months. *Rajab al-murajjab*. And then *Sha'ban al-karim*. *Sha'ban al-karim*... as you have heard, alludes to the Prophet. *Sha'ban al-karim* alludes to the Prophet, but its fifteenth night... the middle night, the fifteenth of Sha'ban... *Shab-e-Barat*, that is upon Mawlana 'Ali. It is very special. ... Now what happened on *Shab-e-Barat*... the fifteenth of Sha'ban, you read the sermons to find out, which night... what things came from above, what things went from below. Things ascend from the earth and things descend from the earth. At what times do they descend? Every night that is great, whether it is the first of Rajab, or the 15th of Rajab, or the 27th of Rajab, or all the nights... they allude to the fact that on this night, on this day, something special happened. It happened in the time of the Prophet, and it has always been, but we don't have the habit of seeing it. Whatever we are obtaining is our own share. Understood? *Al-nasib yusib* (The share reaches [its owner]). As much as there is. So remember, to understand the aspects of the *deen*... we are reading all these things, but the real thing is the *wilayah* of the Commander of the Faithful, which is the special soul. Because *wilayah*... what is it? Fasting, prayer, Hajj, zakat, etc., above all of them... *wilayah* has been placed first in *Da'a'im al-Islam*. If that is not there... and it's not this, that you only believe in 'Ali. You have to believe in 'Ali's progeny as well. The progeny that continued from 'Ali's sons, from Janab Husayn, until today, if they are not affirmed, then your community is void. There is no prayer, no fasting, no Hajj, no zakat. Nothing is there.

So *ikhlas* is a special thing, and the Prophet... people said to him, 'Mawlana, this is... O Messenger of Allah, you have told a very easy thing... that every person... *man qala la ilaha illa Allah dakhala al-jannah*. Every person who is reciting *la ilaha illa Allah* enters paradise.' How is this? So he said, '*Man qala la ilaha illa Allah mukhlisan dakhala al-jannah* (Whoever says there is no god but Allah with sincerity will enter Paradise).' One who recites it with sincerity will enter paradise. *Ikhlas*. Now see what *ikhlas* means. Now *ikhlas*... the people asked, the polytheists of Makkah, the next day they are taking the oath, becoming Muslims, they were completely ignorant... and they came into the Prophet's company. They said, '*Wa ma ikhlasuha?*' (And what is its sincerity?). He said, '*Ada'u huquqiha wa ma'rifatu hududiha* (Fulfilling its rights and knowing its limits).' *Ikhlas* is this: that you fulfill its rights. Prayer, fasting, Hajj, zakat, whatever is established as permanent, perform them. And, *ma'rifatu hudud*. Know the limits. Consider the Prophet as the Prophet, 'Ali as 'Ali, Husayn as Husayn. All the limits that there are, spiritual and physical, adhere to them. This is '*wa ma ikhlasuha*'. Now *ikhlas*. See *ikhlas*. Everywhere it will come, 'recite with sincerity of action.' 'If there is sincerity, then recite.' You are giving the oath, you are giving hand in hand, you are saying the name, so do it with sincerity. In everything, sincerity is given more importance. Sincerity comes from the root *khalasa*. *Khulus* (sincerity) comes from this, *ikhlas* comes from this. And *khulasah* (summary/essence) also comes from this. These are all derivations from the root. So from *khulasah*... now, another thing from the sermons... from the sermons that have come, we know that we have come into the world in the system of waste-matter (*fudlat*). We have come with our bodies, so we want the purification of our bodies. We want to lift this waste-matter. *Suwarat* is a different thing, *Riyayat* is a different thing, and *fudlat* is a different thing. *Fudlat* refers to excess things. Extra. This is... after the soul leaves, the body is useless... to be put in the ground. But what quality is in it? That you... when you read the subjects of the *nawamis* (sermons)... that after death, a person is buried. After three days, his *riyayat* emerge and from there... they pass through. Where all do they go, what happens... *riyayat* are a different thing, *fudlat* are a different thing. So the *riyayat* go to their place, they go to the moon, from the moon they go to another place... The *suwarat* go to the Exalted Boundary (*Hadd-e-'Ali*). But these are all separate items. The *suwarat* go to *Hadd-e-'Ali*, from *Hadd-e-'Ali* to *Hadd-e-'Ali*, up to the *Bab al-Abwab* (Gate of Gates). But the *fudlat*... because it is the lowest thing, that is what needs to be raised. Its purification (*tasfiyah*) must happen. *Tasfiyah*, *khulasah*. How will its purification happen? It is given a place in the earth. It rots, it decomposes. From there, the thing moved, so it comes to the prayer niche of the mosque (*mihrab-e-masjid*). From the prayer niche, it goes to the *miqat* (appointed place). From the *miqat*, it goes to the *matayif* (places of circumambulation). From the *matayif*, it goes to the *mizab-e-rahmah* (gutter of mercy). This is the path. And going there, what does it do? Where did it rise from? All the believers who have passed away in the twelve islands, their *fudlat* are going. Where are they going? The *miqat*... the *miqat* is a path where they don the *ihram* (pilgrim's garb). From there, passing through... they reach the *matayif*. The four *matayif*... look, there are twelve islands. From the twelve islands, things are rising and reaching five *miqats*. So it's less. The circle is shrinking. Now after the five *miqats*, they reach the four *matayif*. The four *matayif* are where we perform the *tawaf* (circumambulation). And in the *tawaf*, the things that spin, from there, the *mizab-e-rahmah* goes. The *mizab-e-rahmah*, how refined it becomes, that it becomes what? From *khama'is* to *zakha'ir*, from *zakha'ir* to *zubad*. *Zubad* is called butter, *zubdah*, meaning the essence, completely soft it becomes. And from there, it goes into the world of constellations (*'alam-e-buruj*). The division of *fudlat*, remember this. That the *fudlat*... what happens to them? Their ascent... that... constellations... how many? The same twelve. It rose from twelve islands. Went into five, from five to four, from four... passing through... one *mizab-e-rahmah*, a special manner of Allah. And the *mizab-e-rahmah* which is attributed to whom? A great *hudud*. It reaches there. From there it goes to the world of constellations. It will take 60 years in each constellation. Our *fudlat*. 30 years like this, anti-clockwise, and clockwise, 30 years. Meaning 60 years in each... in each constellation it has to go. And in this way, when we descend from above, then the purification that has to happen... because in every constellation, some purification happens. It is being chiseled, it is being polished. All things are being washed, being done. So that the second time, Allah creates. So when it comes back and returns... so how many? 30 years, 30 years, i.e., 60 years, and in 12 constellations, it takes 720 years. After 720 years, when they come down again, they will come only into the mineral kingdom. And from mineral they will become plant, from plant they will become animal, they will come to your parents, and from that, you will become a sperm-drop.

And these 720 years... I thought that 720 years are spent above, 280 years in the grave. All this together, after 1000 years, every... from the *da'wah* of Janab Sayyidna Suleiman, after 1000 years, the second coming (*karrat-e-thaniyah*) happens. You have to be repeated. So the point is that 720 years are spent above, and coming down... 720 years were spent in being washed. What is washing? It is the extraction of the essence. *Khulasah*. The same *khulasah*. Now look at *khalasa*, the root. *Kha*, *lam*, *swad*. *Kha* is 600, *lam* is 30, and *swad* is 90. Its sum is 720. So 720 years were spent, and they came down. Then with us... a person was born. And then... this is called coming into the chamber of the mother. And then the *da'wah* was presented, you accepted it, and then you have to move forward. But some move forward, some also fall behind. So this is the system of the *da'wah*, which has meaning for everyone, has expediency for everyone.

So Janab 'Ali, peace be upon him, he also distanced them and then graced them with his intellectual effulgence. He gave permission again. Meaning, the *wasi* of the *da'wah*... meaning, the *wasi* of the *da'wah* bestows upon the people of the *da'wah* from his grace and generosity, but most of the people of the *da'wah* who do not understand these great blessings of the Lord of the *Da'wah*, do not show gratitude.

*Wa qatilu fi sabilillahi wa'lamu anna Allaha sami'un 'alim* (And fight in the cause of Allah and know that Allah is Hearing and Knowing). Fight in the path of Allah and know that Allah is all-hearing, all-knowing. Meaning, to establish the station of the *Wasi*, make *hujjat* (argument/proof). And know that the *Wasi*, whatever you say about you, he is hearing and is aware. Yes. Now Janab 'Ali is hearing. Every Imam hears. He is... Allah is all-aware, similarly the Imam of the time is also all-aware.

*Man dha alladhi yuqridu Allaha qardan hasanan fa yuda'ifahu lahu ad'afan kathiratan* (Who is it that would loan Allah a goodly loan so He may multiply it for him many times over?). Who is it that gives a goodly loan to Allah, so that Allah may increase it for him many times over? Meaning, Allah here addresses the believers and asks, 'Is there anyone among you who will serve the *da'wah* of the *Wasi* without any desire?' Whoever does so, for him is the good news that the *Wasi*, by accepting his selfless service, will raise his rank and station, and will raise it very high. Selfless service. Service of showmanship... selfless service. The service of showmanship is wrong. That is a wrong thing. Showmanship, which is show business, is absolutely not liked in the *da'wah*. Everything should be done with sincerity. The *Wasi*... his selfless service... by accepting it, will raise his rank and station, and will raise it very high.

*Wallahu yaqbidu wa yabsutu wa ilayhi turja'un* (And Allah withholds and grants abundance, and to Him you will be returned). And Allah withholds... He also withholds and He also gives. And to Him you will... to His side you will be returned. Meaning, everything is in the *Wasi*'s hand of power: withholding and giving. It is in their authority. And in every respect, your return is indeed to them. Look, where the verse came, 'to Allah... *turja'un*'... you will be returned to Him. So what is Allah? He is Janab *Wasi*. Now, the *wasi* of the Prophet, Janab 'Ali, peace be upon him. You all know, according to *ta'wil*, that the *naatiq* is in the station of the heavens, and in the station of the earth are the *wasi* of the Prophet. All the *wasi*s there are, they are... *samawati wa al-ard*, *Allahu nuru al-samawati wa al-ard* (Allah is the Light of the heavens and the earth). Allah is the light of the heavens and the earth. What does it mean? It is of the *nutuqa* and the *awsiya'*. And the *wasi* is in the station of the earth. Look, 'you will be returned to the *wasi*'. So everything will be returned to the earth. Wherever it falls from, it will come towards the earth. Now, geographically, in the world too, a special quality has been kept. There is a gravitational force, that everything... the earth pulls it. The way it pulls, similarly the love of 'Ali is pulling. But you have become stubborn. Stubborn means, deviated from the path (*dharam*). You have deviated from the path. You don't believe. So for that reason, you are held back by force. But sometime or another, *yawman ma* (one day), as it is said in the sermon, everyone has to come to the earth. So just as there is a force of attraction in the earth, similarly the Commander of the Faithful is a great magnet of the world, towards whom everyone has to return, provided they abandon their stubbornness. This is the meaning of it.

*Alam tara...* In some places it is also explained... I have explained this before... that I read somewhere that what is meant by 'earth' (*ardh*)? Earth... *iqlim* (clime/region). The *iqlim*... which continent is it? There are seven continents, and seven... in today's time, geographically, it has been stated. But a *da'i* explains that the earth is divided into four parts. One is desert (*sahra*), one is jungle, and one is the ocean, etc., which are the dark regions (*zulumaat*). And only one is a place where there is habitation (*sukoon*), inhabited (*maskun*). Place of habitation. Now look, the caliph... who call themselves caliphs, successors of whom? Of the Prophet. So the first caliph, second, third. These three-three caliphs came, and then the fourth caliph. So what were the three caliphs? They were in the station of the jungle, in the station of the desert. Nothing ever came out of them. Whenever there were questions, the answer could not be formed. Not from Abu Bakr, nor... So what? 'Hey, go ask Abu al-Hasan, what this means.' And this was admitted, 'O Abu al-Hasan, that is... 'Umar said, *Lawla 'Ali la halaka 'Umar* (If it were not for 'Ali, 'Umar would have been destroyed).' 'If 'Ali were not there, 'Umar would have been destroyed. Whom would we have answered? The king of Rome has sent his envoy and is asking questions, we don't have the answers.' So there are some things like this. The earth is there, but farming is not happening on it. The rain is falling, the Prophet's compassion is equal, but the crop that should grow from it is not growing. The one from whom it grows is Mawlana 'Ali, peace be upon him, whose farming took place.

*Alam tara ila al-mala'i min bani isra'ila min ba'di musa idh qalu li nabiyyin lahum ib'ath lana malikan nuqatil fi sabilillah* (Have you not considered the assembly of the Children of Israel after Musa, when they said to a prophet of theirs, "Appoint for us a king that we may fight in the cause of Allah"?). Did you not see this group from the Children of Israel after Musa, when they said to their prophet, 'Appoint a king for us so that we may fight in the path of Allah'? Meaning, O Prophet, do you not know that in a past era, the Jews, after Musa, demanded from the prophet of their time that 'send with us someone who is skilled in debate, in whose leadership we may debate with the enemies of the *da'wah*.'

*Qala hal 'asaytum in kutiba 'alaykum al-qitalu alla tuqatilu* (He said, "Would you perhaps not fight if fighting was prescribed for you?"). The prophet said, 'Is it not possible that if fighting is made obligatory upon you, you will not fight?' Meaning, 'if you are called for exoteric *jihad* and esoteric debate, will you refuse and sit back?'

*Qalu wa ma lana alla nuqatila fi sabilillahi wa qad ukhrijna min diyarina wa abna'ina* (They said, "And why should we not fight in the cause of Allah when we have been driven out from our homes and from our children?"). They said, 'Why should we not fight in the path of Allah, when we have been expelled from our homes and from our children?' Meaning, they said that 'we have gone through the most severe trials. We have been expelled from the regions of our *da'wah*, and we have been separated from our followers. Therefore, we will certainly serve the *da'wah* with zeal and will establish both the exoteric and esoteric *jihad*.' That's enough for now.

...

Let us do two *rak'at* prayer. After making ablution.

<!-- AUG:ibrahim-2015 lecture 3 -->

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful).

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful). "*Alam tara ilal-mala’i min Bani Isra’ila min ba‘di Musa iz qalu li-nabiyyin lahum ub‘ath lana malikan nuqatil fi sabilillah*" (Have you not seen the chieftains of the Children of Israel after Musa, when they said to a prophet of theirs: "Appoint for us a king that we may fight in the way of Allah"). Did you not see the congregation of Bani Isra'il after Musa, who said to their prophet, "Appoint a king for us, then we will perform jihad in the path of Allah"? This is the literal translation of the *ayah* (verse). "Did you not see the congregation of Bani Isra'il after Musa, who said to their prophet, 'Appoint a king for us, then we will perform jihad in the path of Allah'?" Now its meaning, the *ta'wil* (esoteric interpretation), is this: meaning, meaning, O Prophet, do you not know that in a previous era, the Jews, after Musa, demanded from the prophet of their time that he send with us some strong person who can debate, under whose leadership we can engage in argumentation with the enemies of the *da'wah* (mission)? Yes. As I explained yesterday, the Prophet is also called the "Musa of the era." So, all the incidents in the Holy Qur'an, the most numerous incidents are those of Musa, peace be upon him. So, just as it happened in the time of Musa, so too it has happened in the time of our Prophet. And just as it happened with Harun, here it has happened with our master, 'Ali.

"*Qala hal ‘asaytum in kutiba ‘alaykumul-qitalu alla tuqatilu*" (He said: "Would you perhaps, if fighting were prescribed for you, not fight?"). The prophet said, "Is it possible that if jihad is made obligatory upon you, you will not fight?" Meaning, you will be called for the exoteric jihad and the esoteric proof (*hujjat*). Will you then shy away and sit back? "*Qalu wa ma lana alla nuqatila fi sabilillahi wa qad ukhrijna min diyarina wa abna’ina*" (They said: "And why should we not fight in the way of Allah while we have been driven out from our homes and our children?"). They said, "Why would we not fight in the path of Allah, when we have been expelled from our homes and our children... were taken and made distant from us?" Now this is the translation of the *ayah*. "Will we not fight? We will certainly show that we will do jihad." Meaning, meaning, they said, "We have passed through the most severe difficulties. We have been expelled from the regions of our *da'wah*, and we have been separated from the sons of the *da'wah*. Therefore, we will certainly serve the *da'wah* with eagerness, and we will carry out both the exoteric and esoteric jihads."

"*Falamma kutiba ‘alayhimul-qitalu tawallaw illa qalilan minhum, wallahu ‘alimun biz-zalimin*" (But when fighting was prescribed for them, they turned away, except for a few of them. And Allah is Knowing of the wrongdoers). Then when jihad was made obligatory upon them, all but a few turned their faces and fled. And Allah knows the wrongdoers. Meaning, when the time for battle and argumentation actually came, they proved to be cowards and sat with their faces hidden. Only a few supported the Master of the *Da'wah* (*Sahib al-Da'wah*) and fought. This happened during the time of the Prophet Akbar as well. In the battle of Uhud and the battle of Badr, those people who had gained victory, at that time they started looting the spoils of war (*mal-i ghanimat*).
