And they forgot that they had come to perform jihad. At that time, Abu Sufyan came from the rear through the pass, and then he completely turned the tables. He reversed the entire jihad. At that time, Janab Khalifa-i Awwal (the first Caliph) and Khalifa-i Thani (the second Caliph) both fled far away. At that time, the Prophet's instruction came that Janab Abu Bakr was asked, "Abu Bakr, where had you gone?" I mean, they were busy looting the spoils of war, and as soon as they found out that an attack had occurred, they left. And it is mentioned in a book about 'Umar that he came after three days. The Prophet said that you went very far away. So the Prophet's instruction also occurred. So, the point is that they said they would fight, but when the time for battle and argumentation came, they turned their faces away and proved to be cowards. And they sat with their faces hidden. Only a few of them gave support to the *da'wah* and fought. The Imam of the Time (*Imam-i Zaman*) is watching the actions of these wrongdoers well. Yes. This has happened in every era. The Imam of the Time sees their actions. I mean, just as we say Allah is *'Alimun Khabir* (All-Knowing, All-Aware), so too is the Imam of the Time, and so is the Messenger of Allah. Their sight is much better than ours, much higher, and they see who is a true believer. These *Sahaba* (companions) who were there were all for show. And those who were good were good. Those who were bad were bad. Although everyone is called a *Sahabi*, it wasn't so.

"*Wa qala lahum... wa qala lahum nabiyyuhum innallaha qad ba‘atha lakum Taluta malika*" (And their prophet said to them, "Indeed, Allah has appointed Talut as a king for you."). And their prophet said to them, "Behold, Allah has sent Talut to you as a king." Meaning, the Master of the Time (*Sahib-i Zaman*) said, "Behold, we have sent Talut to you after granting him power and strength." "*Qalu anna yakunu lahul-mulku ‘alayna wa nahnu ahaqqu bil-mulki minhu wa lam yu’ta sa‘atan minal-mal*" (They said, "How can he have kingship over us while we are more worthy of kingship than him, and he has not been given an abundance of wealth?"). They said, "How can he be our ruler? We are more deserving of rule and authority. He does not even have much wealth." Meaning, Talut was not pleasing in their eyes. They refused to accept his leadership, saying that we are more deserving than him in the affairs of the *da'wah*. He does not have much knowledge either. In other words, they claimed that we are more learned in the matters of religion than him. Yes. This happened in the time of Nuh as well. Those who were people of wealth (*ashab-i sarwat*) said that Nuh wanders in the markets, buys things, takes goods... how can he be a prophet? We are better than him. So, in every era, the prophet has been rejected like this. The one who is great in the world is seen as not having the authority, and those who are heads of state, or wealthy, have always tried to dominate over religion.

"*Qala innallahastafahu ‘alaykum wa zadahu bastatan fil-‘ilmi wal-jism, wallahu yu’ti mulkahu man yasha’, wallahu wasi‘un ‘alim*" (He said, "Indeed, Allah has chosen him over you and has increased him abundantly in knowledge and physique. And Allah gives His kingdom to whom He wills. And Allah is All-Encompassing, All-Knowing."). The prophet said, "Allah has given him superiority over you, and has blessed him with more power in knowledge and body. And Allah gives His kingdom to whom He wishes. Verily, Allah's knowledge is vast." Meaning, the Imam of the Time did not grant him precedence over you without reason. He is more powerful than you in both exoteric knowledge (*'ilm-i zahir*) and esoteric knowledge (*'ilm-i batin*). And the Imam, peace be upon him, appoints whomever he deems worthy and gives them authority. And he is the one who bestows ranks, support, and confirmation upon the people of his *da'wah* with great generosity. And exoteric and esoteric, in both things he was given superiority. It's not that he was given precedence just like that. He is more powerful than you in both exoteric and esoteric knowledge. This was told to them. Now what happens is that some things are weak in appearance, like in the time of Nuh *alayhi al-salam*, Lut *alayhi al-salam*... it has been like this in every era. And the Imam of the Time appoints whomever he deems worthy. You see, in this, there is *mulk* (kingdom). Like Sulayman was given *mulk*. And Janab Da'ud was given. In some realities, this is the book of *ta'wil* we are reading, in other meanings, *mulk* and *hikmah* (wisdom). "We gave him *mulk* and we gave him *hikmah*." So what is meant by this? The meanings of *mulk* are different and the meanings of *hikmah* are different. I won't mention it here now, because our book is a book of *ta'wil*.

"*Wa qala lahum... wa qala lahum nabiyyuhum inna ayata mulkihi an ya’tiyakumut-tabutu fihi sakinatum mir rabbikum wa baqiyyatum mimma taraka alu Musa wa alu Haruna tahmiluhul-mala’ikah*" (And their prophet said to them, "Indeed, a sign of his kingship is that the Ark will come to you in which is reassurance from your Lord and a remnant of what the family of Musa and the family of Harun had left, carried by the angels."). And their prophet said, "The sign of his kingdom is this: the Ark (*tabut*) will come to you. In it is tranquility from your Lord, meaning *sakinah*, and the remnants of what the progeny of Musa and Harun left behind will be in it. The angels will be carrying it." Meaning, the sign of Talut being the Sultan of the *Da'wah* is that he will teach you the exoteric *shari'ah* in which its esoteric meanings will be hidden. Yes. Just as I explained yesterday, there are some things, for two or three days it has been going on, *tanzil* and *ta'wil*. *Tanzil* is the Prophet's portion, *ta'wil* is Janab 'Ali's, may Allah's blessings be upon him. This *din* has been divided into two parts. When the Prophet spread Islam, at that time there were the rules of *shari'ah*, because there was a large circle of polytheists from which he brought people into the large circle of Islam. So at that time there were the rulings, in the beginning, the rulings of early Islam were presented. But even then there were some such people, like Miqdad, 'Ammar ibn Yasir, Abu Dharr al-Ghifari. These people also knew the inner matters. So it is not that *'ilm-i batin* (esoteric knowledge) was not taught. But in the exoteric sense, some things were told, and it was understood that these are the only rulings. Even today, if someone teaches *Da'a'im al-Islam*, they say, "Oh, what *Da'a'im al-Islam* is he teaching? We are very capable, we are worthy of reading this or that." Have you understood *Da'a'im al-Islam*? *Da'a'im al-Islam* is a basic book. There is no book like it. And which Mawlana Mu'izz authenticated. The Imam of the Time authenticated it. It has been authenticated that this book, until the Day of Judgment, the deeds of all the believers, the reward of this book will increase from that. We think that what is being taught exoterically is just that, but the one who knows, who is a *mufid* (teacher), also knows the esoteric (*batin*). But how much to give to whom, there is a level of aptitude (*istiqdad*). And I mentioned yesterday that *istiqdad* is of different types. It's different for children, different for adults, and different for other people. So on that, the understanding, so it is best that those who are weak of mind, who don't understand, you should feed them. Those who are intelligent understand in one or two sentences. So the job of a *mufid* is that he should assess the aptitude and see who is understanding and who isn't. Yesterday, I mentioned about the *munafiq* (hypocrite). The word *shay'* (thing). See, it will come many times in the Holy Qur'an, what is meant by *shay'*. "*Wa kulla shay’in ahsaynahu fi imamin mubin*" (And all things We have enumerated in a clear Imam). We have placed every thing in the clear Imam. So what is this "thing"? That "thing" is the *mu'min* (believer). About 40 years ago, when I was in Bombay, at that time I was told... I asked Mulla Abbas about it. I myself knew, but for confirmation, I went. He said it is correct. This has come for the *mu'min*. Now it has come in the book that *shay'* is the firm believer, who is a perfect believer (*kamil mu'min*), a true believer, for him *shay'* comes. And the second is *nisf-i shay'* (half-a-thing). *Nisf-i shay'* is the hypocrite. He has one thing in his heart and says another on his tongue. So he is a hypocrite. And *la shay'* (no-thing), that is the *kafir* (disbeliever). That is the *kafir*. So remember that a hypocrite... a hypocrite is one who has one thing in his heart and another on his face or on his tongue. So according to *Da'a'im al-Islam*, the circle of faith is complete only when there is affirmation by the heart (*tasdiq bil-jinan*), and affirmation by the tongue (*iqrar bil-lisan*), and action by the limbs (*'amal bil-arkan*). When the three combine, then it happens. And if these three are not there, two are, the third is not, it means that in the pre-eternal world of spirits (*'alam-i azl*), when it was the world of descent (*hobut*), the intentions made were varied. And there it has been told, it's in *Da'a'im al-Islam* and in larger books too, that even while falling, people's intentions changed. That is called the 'World of Intentions' (*'alam-i niyyat*). While falling, someone was insistent (*musirr*), but slowly became doubtful (*shakk*). Someone who was doubtful became remorseful (*nadim*). When he came down to this physical plane (*surface*), whatever state he was in, his first cycle will be counted. And after that, as the arrangement is, in every cycle, the *da'wah* will be presented to him. If he accepts, he is remorseful; otherwise, he is deserving of hell. So this thing, the hypocrite... the hypocrite has come from *nafaq*. *Nafaq* means a tunnel. And it's called a tunnel, that one thing comes out of one place and leaves from another place. Similarly, for a hypocrite, one thing goes into his ear and comes out of the other ear. And I mentioned two days ago that it is the saying of Mawlana Hasan Shah Rahmatullahi alayhi that what is seen with the eyes is truth, and what is heard with the ears is falsehood. And the distance between the two is four fingers. We think this is so-and-so's saying, that one's saying. It is the saying of Mawlana Hasan Shah Rahmatullahi alayhi.

"*Inna fi zalika la’ayatal lakum in kuntum mu’minin*" (Indeed, in that is a sign for you, if you are believers). Verily, in this is a sign for you, provided you are believers. Meaning, if you understand and accept the station of Talut, then in it is a great indication. "*Falamma fasala Talutu bil-junudi qala innallaha mubtalikum bi-nahar*" (And when Talut set out with the soldiers, he said, "Indeed, Allah will test you with a river"). Then when Talut took his army and left, he told them, "Allah will test you with a river." Meaning, when Talut went out for battle with the group of believers, he told them that first the Imam of the Time will test you in a confrontation with a powerful opponent who is a claimant of knowledge, is arrogant and sharp-tongued, a logician and speaks very well. "*Fa-man shariba minhu fa-laysa minni wa man lam yat‘amhu fa-innahu minni illa manigh-tarafa ghurfatan bi-yadih*" (Then whoever drinks from it is not of me, and whoever does not taste it is indeed of me, except for one who takes a scoop with his hand). So whoever is quenched by it is not mine, and whoever does not even taste it is surely mine, except for one who takes a handful with his hand. Meaning, whoever among you is inclined towards that claimant, and after listening carefully to his words, leans towards him, he is not mine. And whoever remains steadfast is mine. Except for that person who is somewhat impressed by his speech but, by benefiting from his *Hadd-i 'Ali* (high-ranking missionary), escapes its effect and remains steadfast, he is not liable to be seized.

"*Fa-sharibu minhu illa qalilan minhum*" (But they drank from it, except for a few of them). So, except for a few, all the soldiers drank from it. Meaning, the soldiers, disobeying the command, listened carefully to the words of that sharp-tongued claimant, which were entirely based on falsehood, and became his followers. See, what is true is also bitter, and falsehood is sharp-tongued. And a sharp-tongued person, there are many different stories you will read in *Asas al-Ta'wil*, the whole book is full of stories about how people were deceived. Even during the Prophet's own era, when people sat in the Ka'bah, they would conspire against the Prophet. And Allah the Almighty says: "*Wa makaru wa makarallah, wallahu khayrul-makirin*" (And they plotted, and Allah plotted. And Allah is the best of plotters). O my prophet, these people plot, and you should remember that Allah is the greatest plotter. Allah is the greatest. What kind of statement is this? Allah is the greatest plotter. Meaning, He will turn the tables on them in such a way that they won't know what hit them. And it happened that way. Meaning, for thirty years, Janab 'Ali remained silent. This is the *ta'wil* of our thirty-day fast, that for thirty years we were kept away from the Imamate. 'Ali remained silent for thirty years, despite having all the power, yet you remained silent. Why? It was Allah's will and the Prophet's matter. And you remained silent. The Prophet's *wasi* (successor) kept showing kindness, and these people kept plotting. As many as there were, the First Caliph, what kinds of incidents are there, that before the Prophet's eyes closed, what was said, in the field of Saqifah, in the gathering of Saqifah, what all happened? Saqifah is a cursed place. And it is behind the wall of the Prophet's Mosque. So remember that meetings... these meetings used to happen there. And Marwan, Abu Sufyan, all of them used to sit there. And they were all against the Prophet. I mean, they had accepted the Prophet because there was no one as truthful and trustworthy as him. So they had to accept him. And I have mentioned this before, that how the First Caliph said that "we accept you, but after you, please take care of me." It is in the books. So this has happened three times. I have narrated this before, that three... in the time of Janab Khalilullah (Ibrahim) it also happened. In the time of Ibrahim Khalilullah, Azar, he was an idol-worshipper. Meaning, he was from the *du'at* of the time. Janab Ibrahim Khalil was small, six, seven, eight years old. You used to give them the idols, you didn't even know. But as soon as divine guidance (*rushd*) came to you, a revelation came from Allah, and you were made a prophet, you left the house. He said, "Azar, now leave my... Nuh's *shari'ah*, and my *din* is better, come into it." So what did he say? Azar... *shiba-i Azari* is what idol-making is called. *Shiba-i Azari*. He used to make idols. The idol-breaker (*but-shikan*) is Mawlana 'Ali, may Allah's blessings be upon him. The last idol... the tradition of idol-making, that was said to be Azar's, and he was the maternal uncle (*nana*) of Janab Ibrahim Khalilullah. The moment the Prophet said, Ibrahim said, "Come into my *din*," at that time he said, "*ankara was-takbara*" (he denied and was arrogant). He denied and was arrogant. "*ankara was-takbara*." At that time, Janab Ibrahim said, "*haza 'aduwwullah, fa-tabarra'a minhu*" (this is an enemy of Allah, so I disassociate from him). This is an enemy of Allah, so disassociation from him is necessary. And at that very moment, he left the house. And after that, you know the incidents, that he went to a fair, and that happened, and this... he was a young man of your age, twelve, fourteen, fifteen years old. At that time, he left the house. Just as that happened, here in the time of the Prophet, now see, there Azar said, "*ankara was-takbara*." He denied and was arrogant. Here, during the Prophet's time, they, after accepting the Prophet, entered the *din*. The First Caliph. Who were they? They were from the *Hudud-i din* (ranks of the religion) of the previous era, the era of 'Isa. They were minor ones. But they were coming from there, they were very old. He said that I accept you. They accepted him out of compulsion. There is only this much difference. But later, in private, he said to the Prophet, "After you, please take care of me, I should become the Caliph." At that time, from the Prophet's mouth came, "*haza 'aduwwullah, fa-tabarra'a minhu*" (this is an enemy of Allah, disassociate from him). This is an enemy of Allah, disassociation from him is necessary. Then the *da'i* writes that a third time, in our era, the one who was arrogant (*istikbar*) and proud (*kibr*) was 'Abdullah ibn Mahfuz. And Syedna Shakir Sahib writes, Syedna 'Ali ibn Husayn writes, that there is one Pharaoh, one Haman, one Shaddad, all are of different types. One was a statue of greed, one of lust, one of jealousy, and one of pride. So the pride was that of 'Abdullah ibn Mahfuz. He was a very high-ranking *hadd*. And in Yemen, he was considered the greatest *hadd*. And there was no one above him. He was capable and everything. But what happened? In the end, he became *jahil wa masihi* (ignorant and Christian). He turned against Syedna Sulayman Shah. He used to accept the *Hudud*, everything. But when he saw that the *da'wah* of India is in their control, the entire property is in the hands of the *Da'ud al-Hadd*. All the property, accounts, even the *chaliyan* (areca nuts) and whatever else there was, everything was in the hands of the *Da'ud al-Hadd*. He immediately turned. This is a matter of the year 1000 AH. In the year 1000 AH, this change occurred. So at that time, too, the *da'i* writes that he "*ankara was-takbara*," so he is "*haza 'aduwwullah, fa-tabarra'a minhu*." From him too, disassociation is necessary. So the matter of *bara'ah* (disassociation) is that we are 'Ali-walas because of this, not just that we accept 'Ali, but that those who were against the *da'wah*, who became Caliphs against the rightful one, disassociation from them is also necessary. If this is not in your heart, then your love for 'Ali is not complete. The *walayat-i mawla* is complete only when, when water is filled, first the dirty things are taken out of it. This is why "La ilaha illallah" (There is no god but Allah) is there. First you negate, then you come to affirmation.

...did not correct and confirm his false speech from their *hudud-i din* (ranks of the religion). He dominated them with his wrong logic and false philosophy. Their intellect and understanding could not grasp it. However, a few intelligent people with firm faith were saved from his evil. "*Falamma jawazahu huwa wallazina amanu ma‘ahu qalu la taqata lanal-yawma bi-Jaluta wa junudih*" (So when he had crossed it, he and those who believed with him, they said, "There is no power for us today against Jalut and his soldiers"). When Talut and his believing companions crossed from there, they said, "Today we do not have the strength to fight Jalut and his army." Meaning, that remaining small group of believers lost heart and said, "At this time, we do not have the strength or power to confront the enemy of the Imam and his helpers." Jalut is the enemy of Talut. Here too, the same thing happened. The battle of Nahrwan, the battle of Siffin, and the battle of Jamal. These battles that took place were with the *kafirin* (disbelievers), not with the *mushrikin* (polytheists). The battles with the *mushrikin* were Uhud, Badr, and whatever others there are, Tabuk, Khandaq and Khaybar. But the ones that were with the *kafirin*... *kafirin* means those who committed *kufr* (disbelief) after having faith. I mean, they came into Islam and then changed. They are the *kafirin*. And Janab 'Ali fought against them. Three battles are famous. And the reference to this is that one is *mariqin*, one is *nakisin*, and one is *qasitin*. The three. The *Nasr-i 'Aziz* that we recite is for this reason. And you see, this *Nasr-i 'Aziz* that we recite, this is the only difference between us and the opponents, who are our opponents in the *da'wah*. They do not have the prayer of *Nasr-i 'Aziz*. And in it, the *mariqin*, etc., are there. This prayer says that all the *nakisin* are these very people. Sayyidna Sulayman Shah did not consider them. The *nakisin* are the same, the *qasitin* and the *mariqin* are these very people. In that era, there were three types. Those who were with Mu'awiya were separate, they were Kharijites. There were three types. Here, all three of them have come into one.

"*Qalal-lazina yazunnuna annahum mulaqullah, kam min fi’atin qalilatin ghalabat fi’atan kathiratan bi-iznillah, wallahu ma‘as-sabirin*" (Said those who were certain that they would meet Allah, "How often has a small company overcome a large company by permission of Allah. And Allah is with the patient"). Those who had the thought... or conviction in their minds that they had to appear before Allah, said, "No matter, many a small group has overcome a large group by the command of Allah. And Allah is with those who are patient." Meaning, those who had faith in the support and help of Mawlana Imam alayhi al-salam did not hesitate and said, "If we are few, what is the sorrow? Mawlana Imam will help us and grant us victory over the enemy. Mawlana Imam keeps helping those who are patient in difficulties." That's all for today.

[Arabic Prayer]

<!-- AUG:ibrahim-2015 lecture 4 -->

A little earlier. Talut, Jalut. The incident of Talut... 143... 144... 124... 124...

*Qala alladheena yadhunnoona annahum mulaaqul laahi kam min fi'atin qaleelatin ghalabat fi'atan katheeratan bi idhnil laah, wallaahu ma'as saabireen* (Said those who were certain that they would meet Allah, "How many a small company has overcome a large company by permission of Allah. And Allah is with the patient").

Those people who were certain that they had to appear before Allah said, "It is no matter, many small groups have overcome large, numerous groups by Allah's command, and Allah is with those who are patient." Meaning, those who were certain of the support (*ta'id*) and aid (*imdad*) of Mawlana Imam (peace be upon him), they did not hesitate and said, "If we are few, what sorrow is there? Mawlana Imam will help us and grant us victory over the enemy."

This has been stated, it is from the time of Prophet Da'ud. Talut and Jalut were two individuals; Talut was good, and Jalut was bad. A war took place with them, and Allah granted victory. So how was it? A small group prevailed over a large group. It has always been so, that truth, *al-haqqu ya'lu wa la yu'la* (truth rises and is not risen upon). It appears weak but later it is victorious. And in the end, when the world is about to end, at that time too it will happen that the evil ones (*ashrar*), the bad people, will be defeated. The misguided and the arrogant, those people and the doubters, and the deputies (*na'ibeen*) will be victorious in the time of the *Qa'im* (peace and blessings be upon him) and in the time of Imam Mahdi. So, remember this, that time is always *tilka al-ayyamu nudawiluha baynan-nas* (these are the days which We alternate among people). Time has never remained the same. It has always kept changing. And sometimes there is a decline in religion (*deen*), and sometimes there is an increase. So remember that these people who thought, those who were with the truth, they said, "If we are few, there is no sorrow." Just as today, our community (*jama'at*) is a very small community. And we believe that the Imam of the Time is going to help us. He will help us in every matter. Meaning, those who have certainty (*yaqin*) of the support and aid of the Imam of the Time (peace and blessings be upon him). Having certainty is the most important thing. If we are certain that we will receive support, then we are not going to be defeated. They did not hesitate and said that if we are few, what is the sorrow? Mawlana Imam will help us. And this is... Allah, wherever Allah's name has come, it is the Imam of the Time. This is the esoteric interpretation (*ta'wil*) of Sayyidna Husayn Jalaluddin. It is a book of *ta'wil*.

*Wa lamma barazoo li-Jaluta wa junoodihee qaaloo Rabbana afrigh 'alainaa sabran wa thabbit aqdaamanaa wansurnaa 'alal qawmil kaafireen* (And when they went forth to [face] Jalut and his soldiers, they said, "Our Lord, pour upon us patience and make firm our feet and give us victory over the disbelieving people"). And at the time when they came out to face Jalut and his army, they prayed, "O our sustainer, grant us complete patience, make our feet firm, and give us victory against the community of disbelievers." Meaning, when they finally gathered the courage and became ready to debate with Jalut and his companions, they requested from the Imam (peace be upon him) the prayer for victory and dominance for themselves. That, "Grant us patience at the time of confrontation. May our feet not waver, and grant us victory over your enemies."

*Fa hazamoohum bi idhnil laahi wa qatala Daawoodu Jaaloota wa aataahul laahul mulka wal hikmata wa 'allamahoo mimmaa yashaaa'* (So they defeated them by permission of Allah, and Da'ud killed Jalut, and Allah gave him the kingship and wisdom and taught him from that which He willed). So they defeated the enemies by the command of Allah, and Da'ud killed Jalut, and Allah gave him the... Prophet Da'ud is a very great prophet, who was the father of Prophet Sulayman. Both have a special rank as prophets. The Zabur was revealed to Prophet Da'ud. So remember, Prophet Da'ud and Prophet Sulayman... We have 124,000 prophets, but there is a distinction among them. *Faddalna ba'dahum 'ala ba'd* (We preferred some of them over others). The rank of Musa is different, Ibrahim's is different, and Adam's is different. The rank of each one is different. All are called prophets (*Nabi*). Now, the meaning of *Nabi* is, those who understand, who sit in lessons, they know that *Nabi* is the lowest among the ranks of the *asasi* (foundations). And higher than that are the *Rasul*s (messengers). So, a *Rasul* is one who is given a *risalah* (message). They have a *risalah*. They have a magazine from Allah. It is revealed, and they act upon it. *Nabi* is derived from *naba'*, which means one who gives news. A *Nabi* comes and gives news of the one who will come after him. So, *Nabi* is a lower rank among the ranks of the *asasi*, and after that is the rank of *risalat* (messengership). These two ranks are what are called initiatory (*ibtida'i*) ranks. Remember that they are called initiatory ranks. They are not the real masters (*malik*). The *malik*s are the Imams of the Time. So, remember that as many *Rasul*s as there are... there are *Rasul*s and *Nabi*s and so on. But among them, those who are the *Nutuqa'* (law-giving prophets)—Adam, Nuh, Ibrahim, Musa, Isa, and the Prophet Muhammad—their ranks are different. As it has been said, *faddalna ba'dahum 'ala ba'd*. And not a single one is of the same rank. All are called *Nabi*s, but Allah's statement is that one was given preference over another. Among them, there is a rank that is the rank of Da'ud and Sulayman. They were granted *mulk* (kingship) and *hikmah* (wisdom). I have mentioned yesterday that they are very high up. What is the meaning of *hikmah*, and what is the meaning of *mulk*? They were given *mulk*, which means they were given kingship. But it is not that kingship; it is something else. That they were granted *mulk* and *hikmah*. Now, what is it here? That a small community prevailed, and Da'ud, Prophet Da'ud, killed whom? `qatala Daawoodu Jaaloota` (Da'ud killed Jalut). Prophet Da'ud is the subject (*fa'il*). If we read this incorrectly, and say `Daawooda`, then it becomes a sin. That Jalut killed Da'ud. So, in the Holy Quran, wherever such verses have come, in many Qurans it is written at the back that if this mistake of vowels is made, it is not just considered a mistake, but it becomes a sin.

And he was given *mulk* and *hikmah*. See, both *mulk* and *hikmah* were granted. What does that mean? I just told you that prophets have initiatory ranks. But when *hikmah* is given, its meaning is something else. That they have many... from the initiatory ranks... they have been promoted, and what has been given? It has come in the great books.

Meaning, this small group won, and Da'ud finished off Jalut. Mawlana Imam made him the master of the affair of the mission (*amr al-da'wat*), taught him esoteric knowledge (*'ilm al-batin*), and gave him the teaching of spiritual gnosis (*ma'rifat-e-ruhaniyyah*). Yes.

*Wa law laa daf'ul laahin naasa ba'dahum bi ba'dil la fasadatil ardu wa laakinnal laaha dhoo fadlin 'alal 'aalameen* (And if it were not for Allah checking some people by means of others, the earth would have been corrupted, but Allah is full of bounty to the worlds). And if Allah did not repel the evil of people among themselves through one another, the earth would be ruined. But Allah is indeed bountiful to the people of the world. Meaning, if Mawlana Imam did not repel the evil of the wicked in this manner, then the earth of the mission (*da'wat*) would become empty and be destroyed. Yes. But it is connected to the Imam of the Time. We don't leave everything to governments, but rather it is the Imam of the Time who is our helper. And a believer should always have on his face or in his mouth this prayer: `Allahumma... Ya Mawlana Muhammadin Ali, aghithni` (O Allah... O our master Muhammad bin Ali, help me!). Help us. In every era, in every age, corruption has spread greatly in the world. This statement should always be with us: `Ya Mawlana Muhammadin Ali, ya Imam al-Zaman, aghithni` (O our master Muhammad bin Ali, O Imam of the Time, help me!). `Aghithni` must be said. Our Imam of the Time is our savior.

*Tilka aayaatul laahi natloohaa 'alaika bil haqqi wa innaka la minal mursaleen* (These are the verses of Allah which We recite to you in truth. And indeed, you are from among the messengers). And we see that the world is being greatly destroyed. If the world did not have the charity of the Imam of the Time, the world would have been destroyed.

These are the verses of Allah, which are true. We recite them to you because you are indeed from among the messengers. Yes. Now, they are from among the messengers (*Rasul*). You are from among the *Rasul*s. See, here the rank of *Rasul* is mentioned. That Da'ud and Sulayman are *Rasul*s and also *Nabi*s. So, one who is a *Rasul* is also a *Nabi*. But not every *Nabi* is a *Rasul*. How many *Rasul*s are there? There are 313. And there are 124,000 *Anbiya'* (prophets). In the 124,000, the distinction that has been made, how do we make it? That the 124,000 are all *Anbiya'*, and among them, 313, which is the number that participated in the Battle of Badr, that number is of the *Rasul*s. So, *Rasul*... now, among the common people, neither *Rasul* is known nor *Nabi* is known. They say *Nabi* and *Rasul* have the same rank. Sometimes they say *Rasul*, sometimes Allah says this. But it is not so. The rank of the *Rasul*s is higher. One to whom *risalat* (a message) was given. They were not sent just to give news. Now, those who are higher than them are called *Nutuqa'*. There are six *Nutuqa'*. So far, six have passed. The seventh *Natiq* has not yet come. Adam (peace be upon him) is the first, Nuh (peace be upon him) is the second, the third is Ibrahim, the fourth is Musa, then Isa, and after that, the Prophet. So the sixth rank is the Prophet; you are the sixth *Nabi* (peace and blessings be upon him). And the seventh, who is the Seal of the Prophets (*Khatim al-Anbiya'*), is the person of the *Qa'im* (peace be upon him). Now, among them, those who are called *Nutuqa'*, the *Nutuqa'* have a large circle, which... in the world of essence (*'alam-e-azal*), they carried out such missions that a large circle came under their leadership. And in that, Christianity came, Judaism came, all who came... those who followed Musa were there, in the millions, in the billions, so here Judaism also spread as a very large religion. And the second is Christianity. That the followers of Prophet Isa are all followers of Isa. But remember this, we also say Isa (peace be upon him). Our argument (*hujjat*) is this, that we who are Sulaymani or are people of truth (*ahl-e-haqq*), we believe in Isa, we believe in Musa, we believe in Ibrahim, we believe in all the prophets. And why do we believe? Because the Prophet affirmed that all the 124,000 who came were sent by Allah. We believe in them. Now, remember this, that among the *Nutuqa'*, the *Nutuqa'* have a special circle, which is a very large umbrella, and under them, millions and billions of people exist. And we believe that Isa was also correct, Musa was also correct, all were right in their own time. But when Musa came, the law (*shari'at*) of Ibrahim ended. When Isa came, the law of Musa ended. So their work is not just law-giving (*natiqiyat*), but they are also abrogators (*nasikh*). They are abrogators; they end one law and spread their own law. And in the same way, our claim is that when the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) came, who is the last *Nabi* and the last *Rasul*, he spread his own law, and he abrogated Christianity. And not just Christianity, all of them from Adam to Isa, as many prophets as there were... and its esoteric meaning is something else again, that the sum of all of them gathered in your person. If the sum had not gathered, you would not be called *Sayyid al-Mursalin* (Master of the Messengers). The meaning of *Sayyid al-Mursalin* is that every prophet was absorbed into you. So, the *Nutuqa'* have a special rank. Now, how many *Nutuqa'* have there been? Six. So now, in the 124,000, what was the division? There were 313. Among the 313, six are the prophets who are *Nutuqa'*. And among the six, five are the *Ulul 'Azm* prophets. Five are *Ulul 'Azm* prophets, who are higher than them. And they are, leaving Adam aside, Nuh, Ibrahim, Musa, Isa, and the Prophet. These five are called *Ulul 'Azm* prophets, who had some steadfastness. And they had the courage to spread their *deen*. They were very effective. Now, remember this, that the five *Ulul 'Azm* prophets are the archetypes (*mamsul*) of our five prayers. The five prayers we perform—*Fajr*, *Zuhr*, *'Asr*, *Maghrib*, *Isha*—their five archetypes have been mentioned. This is because it is the month of Ramadan, we are praying, so we should understand the prayer, why it is and what is the archetype behind it. There are many archetypes, but the one that is easy and understandable is that the five who are the *Ulul 'Azm* prophets are our... these are the five *Ulul 'Azm*. Meaning, it starts with Nuh. Nuh, among the *Ulul 'Azm* prophets, is the greatest prophet. The first one. And after that, the rank of Ibrahim was raised even higher. And how was it raised? That... `wa ja'alna Ibrahima imama` (And We made Ibrahim an Imam). We made Ibrahim an Imam. So he was neither a *Nabi* nor a *Rasul*... he was a *Rasul* and a *Nabi* before, and after that a time came when he was also granted *Imamah*. If that had not happened, the lineage that has come to the Prophet, your lineage, it started from Prophet Ibrahim. So, Mawlana Ibrahim Khalilullah is... except for the Prophet, among all the prophets that have passed in the world, the rank of Ibrahim is the greatest. Mawlana Ibrahim Khalilullah, who is the friend of Allah (*Khalilullah*), so the other is the beloved of Allah (*Habibullah*), the prophet of Allah who is the beloved, the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him). So remember that these five who are the *Ulul 'Azm* prophets, if we make a distinction among them as to who is the greater prophet, it is Prophet Ibrahim and the person of the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him). And even between them, if we distinguish, the Prophet is *Sayyid al-Mursalin*, *Khatim al-Anbiya'*, and among all creation, the greatest being is your noble self.

Yes.

Meaning, these individuals whose story we have narrated are Allah's *a'immah* (Imams). We are telling you their true stations and ranks. Your *Lahut* is the essence of their *Lahut*s, and your *Nasut* is the sum of their *Nasut*s. They all came and gathered in your person. That is why you are their appointed time (*miqat-e-ma'lum*). And you... As I told you just now, that the rank of Da'ud and Sulayman was also raised, and many things are in it. They had a connection with *Imamah*. Now these are very high matters, maybe you won't understand, but remember that *ibtida'* (initiation) and *mustaqarr* (permanent)—these two are the streams through which Allah... our progress will happen. So, *mustaqarr* Imams are different, the *mustaqarri* lineage is different, the *ibtida'i* lineage is different. And in the *ibtida'i* lineage, sometimes such lofty individuals emerge, like our *Nutuqa'*, that their rank is very high. And their esoteric interpretations are also many and lengthy. But remember this, that all the work that is happening in the world, from Adam until now, all the wars that have occurred, whether of Jalut or Talut, or of Qabil and Habil, and others too, at the time of Fir'awn it happened. Fir'awn was against Musa; he came before Musa. Namrud came before Prophet Ibrahim. And against Prophet Nuh also they came. All the prophets... an opposition (*zidd*) has always been created against them. Wherever a friend of Allah (*wali Allah*) was, an opposition to Allah was also created. An opposition to the *Nabi* was created. So remember that if this opposition did not exist, their value would not be known. Value would not be known. And what is Allah? *Al-haqqu ya'lu wa la yu'la* (Truth rises and is not risen upon). This is Allah's promise, that everything will happen, but Allah's word will remain جاری (current). It will go on until the Day of Judgment. It happens. Why does decline happen in *deen*? All the believers who are being born in the world, they have their characteristics, their substances (*mahwiyyat*), the system of their lineage (*nasab*), the system of their marriage, and all the things... the teachings. The bigger the circle becomes, the more corruption starts spreading there. So remember that we consider Isa to be Isa, we believe it. We believe Isa is a prophet. But we don't *follow* him. This is the difference. Between us and the Christians. We believe in them all equally. The prophet has affirmed them, so we have to believe that Prophet Musa was also true, and Prophet Isa was also true. Because their... Now here, see, that which we have, when we perform *tawaf* (circumambulation) of the *Tabutullah*, in the *tawaf*, there are four corners from where we turn. So in turning, one... the various... there are... *Rukn Yamani* (Yemeni Corner), *Rukn 'Iraqi* (Iraqi Corner), *Rukn Shami* (Syrian Corner), all these. We say *Allahu Akbar* (Allah is the greatest). So among them, two are the *Rukn Shami* and the *Rukn 'Iraqi*. These two are based on whom? They are on Prophet Musa and Prophet Isa. So we greet them from a distance. We don't... just as there is the command to kiss the Black Stone, we don't do that, but rather... `Bismillah, Allahu Akbar` (In the name of Allah, Allah is the greatest), we say. From a distance. This is because we do not follow them. And our argument (*hujjat*) is that the Christian people... we believe in the Prophet and we also believe in Isa. They do not believe in our Prophet. That's the difference.

Now, see, "because you are indeed from the messengers." Meaning, these individuals whose story we have narrated are Allah's *a'immah* (Imams) of the religion, and we narrate their true stations and ranks. The true ranks that are narrated, only the people of knowledge know what their rank is. Your *Lahut* is the essence of their *Lahut*s. The essence of their *Lahut*s... those who have studied, those who know the terminology, they know what it means. What is *Lahut* and what is *Nasut*? *Nasut*, in the language of philosophy, is that which is visible. And *Lahut* is an abstract form, which is not visible. Your *Nasut* is the sum of their *Nasut*s. They all came and gathered in your person. This gathering and separating, this has always been happening in the world. From Adam to here, things meet, and many lights and secrets meet, and then they get divided. So wherever they are divided, wherever they are gathered, a great man comes there. And to understand him is our job. It is the blessing of this month of Ramadan that these topics, which Sayyidna Husayn Jalaluddin has written, we are fortunate enough to read them.

Is it done?

Please read.

Is it finished? No, it's done. Did it get done? Okay... No, it didn't... Read a little... Let's finish it quickly.

Okay.

Look, this `Tilka al-rusul` which is the third *juz'* (part), its... `Rawdat al-Mustafid` is the book of Sayyidna Husayn Jalaluddin, and it was written 250 years ago. We did not have its translation. The translation happened just now, 15-20 years ago, during the time of Hazrat Ziyai Sahab. Mulla Amir Sahab translated it. The desire that was in our hearts, that was in the minds of the believers, that we do not have any *tafsir* (commentary) of the Holy Quran, that is a wrong notion. It was written 250 years ago. And it has happened before that too, but this systematic book is what Sayyidna Husayn Jalaluddin has written. We couldn't read its translation, so we didn't understand what the meaning of which verse is. Now, wherever the word `ya'ni` (meaning) comes, the real *ta'wil* (esoteric interpretation) has been explained there. Otherwise, the translation has been given.

Go ahead.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Wa bihi nasta'in, wa 'alayhi natawakkal* (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. And in Him we seek help, and upon Him we rely). All praise is for Allah alone, who has opened the locks of difficult sciences for the servants of His saints (*awliya'*), and from their tongues, has revealed the hidden secrets of the Shari'ah. I praise Him and bear witness that there is no god but Him. He is living, magnificent, supreme, and I send blessings upon His messenger Sayyidna Muhammad and his *wasi* and both their progeny, who are the shining stars and constellations of the *deen*. And send blessings upon the Imam of the era, the Master of the Time, who is appointed for the appointed day. And send salutations upon his *hujjat*s and *du'at* who are the pool of his sciences. And the sealed cups of secrets. O group of seekers of the science of reality! May Allah open the locks of deep sciences for me and for you, and may He free me and you from the captivity and shackles of being deaf and dumb. The sciences that Mawlana Imam and his two *da'i*s have explained, you have heard them. From his blessed tongue, those symbols of the Quran were told to you which are hidden from the common people, and which only the people of intellect can understand. Now you will hear those subtle points of that same series which have been connected with it. Allah is the seeker of help from His saints.

Now, Mawlana Imam and his two *da'i*s. The two *da'i*s that Sayyidna Husayn Jalaluddin has mentioned are Sayyidna Ali bin Sayyidna Sulayman and Sayyidna Ibrahim. These were the two *da'i*s in his time. And it is mentioned that this was written by the command of Sayyidna Ali Sahab. Sayyidna Ali bin Sayyidna Sulayman. And when two or three *surah*s were done, after Surah Baqarah, from where Surah Nisa and others came, at that time Sayyidna Husayn said that this will not be possible for me. This work will not be done. It is a very lofty matter. He said, "It will be done by you." Sayyidna Ali Sahab said. He pleaded a lot, but Sayyidna Ali Sahab did not agree. And he said, "This work cannot be done by me." At that time, Sayyidna Ali Sahab commanded his brother Ibrahim to... His brother Ibrahim had a very special rank. He was among the learned (*'ilmiyyin*) and was a great scholar. He has written very special books. So, Sayyidna... his name was Sarim. Sayyidna Ibrahim. He was given the command. So, the two or three *juz'*s, or the three *surah*s, its translation... the *ta'wil* that has been explained is the *ta'wil* of Sayyidna Ibrahim. Remember this. And it is very lofty. In it, the matter of inheritance, how much share should go to whom, all that is explained. Now we are starting the third *juz'*. `Tilka al-rusul`.

This is the exegesis of the third *juz'*. Whoever contemplates and reflects upon it will attain his share of blessings. And its deep, pleasant effects... and debate about the treasures and troves of its subtle meanings.

*Tilka al-rusulu faddalna ba'dahum 'ala ba'd* (Those are the messengers; We preferred some of them over others). These are the messengers, among whom We have given preference to some over others. Meaning, here the Exalted Creator first mentions the messengers, and then He explains that they are not all of one rank. Not all are of one rank. As I have told you, the one... yes. But their ranks are higher and lower.

*Minhum man kallamal laahu wa rafa'a ba'dahum darajaat* (Among them are those to whom Allah spoke, and He raised some of them in degree). Among them, there is one with whom Allah spoke, and He raised the ranks of some. Meaning, Mawlana Imam al-Zaman made Musa his interlocutor (*kalimah*) and, by virtue of being the fourth, He gave him... Now, you should understand that as much of the Holy Quran as has come... the Holy Quran is before us, and in it all the stories, all the incidents, are of the prophets and messengers. There is no mention of any Imam. Why is this? It is because this is the era of concealment (*dawr al-satr*). And in this, the book that has come contains the stories of all the messengers. And how are they? You can take out and read *Asas al-Ta'wil* or read *Qutb al-Najah*. Any book you read, no verse of the Quran tells where the Imam is. But all the things that the people of the initiatory level are doing, whether Da'ud does it or Sulayman does it, Musa does it or Isa does it, or they come before Fir'awn, or Ibrahim... so all the people of the initiatory rank, the command is coming to them from the Imam. And the Imam is hidden. This is the era of concealment. This is our era of concealment. This whole setup, understand it, that in the era of concealment, the Imam will always be hidden, and the *Nabi* (prophet) will be manifest in his time. The *Nabi* is, the *Rasul* is, all of them. And in today's time, after the Prophet, after the Prophet, there is no prophet after me, `la nabiyya ba'di`. The Prophet's saying is that there is no prophet after me. Now, all those who are of lower ranks, we call them *da'i*s. The *da'i* has come in its place. Let's go.

*Wa aatayna 'Isa ibna Maryamal bayyinaati wa ayyadnaahu bi roohil qudus* (And We gave Jesus, the son of Mary, clear proofs, and We supported him with the Holy Spirit). And We gave Jesus, son of Mary, clear miracles, and supported him through the Holy Spirit. Meaning, We gave him the power to explain esoteric meanings clearly, and endowed him with the sum of subtleties, which increased his inner strength and elevated him in rank.

*Wa law shaa'al laahu maqtatalal ladheena mim ba'dihim mim ba'di ma jaa'at humul bayyinaat* (And if Allah had willed, those who came after them would not have fought each other after the clear proofs had come to them). And if Allah had willed, those who came after them would not have fought among themselves after receiving clear signs and proofs. Meaning, if the Exalted One had willed, people would have known the station of the *wasi* (successor) and, after knowing it, would not have fought for the seizure of worldly leadership. And it has always been that the messengers and them have been rejected. In the time of Nuh, you see, in every era. But the real rejection happens with whom? After the *Nabi*, for the *Wasi*. It has always been that the disputes are for the *Wasi*. After the Prophet, the same thing happened as happened in the time of Musa. That is why one of the titles of the Prophet is that he is called the Musa of the era. And Harun is in the place of Prophet Ali. The Prophet himself has stated that, "O Ali, you are to me as Harun was to Musa."

*Wa laakinikhtalafoo faminhum man aamana wa minhum man kafar, wa law shaa'al laahu maqtataloo wa laakinnal laaha yaf'alu ma yureed* (But they differed, and some of them believed and some of them disbelieved. And if Allah had willed, they would not have fought, but Allah does what He intends). But they differed, so some among them believed and some disbelieved. And if Allah had willed, they would not have engaged in fighting. But Allah does as He wills. Meaning, despite the clear proofs, they differed regarding the *wasi* and the *a'immah*. Some accepted, and some denied. If the Exalted One wanted to stop it, this would not have happened. But since He wants to distinguish between the impure (*khabith*) and the pure (*tayyib*), that is why it happened by His will and intention. See, if the bad did not exist, then everyone in the world would be born good. All would become believers. It hasn't happened that way. But it is necessary to separate the impure from the pure. That wherever a messenger has appeared, a friend of Allah (*wali Allah*) has appeared, a bad person has also appeared before them. And this method has always been there. And history has always been repeated. From Adam to here, you read the incidents, in the people of every era, in this way, the prophets have been rejected. They have been tormented. Our Prophet was tormented the most. And for this, in every era, there has been a devil (*shaytan*). Fir'awn came before Musa, Namrud came before Ibrahim. In every era, one devil has been there. And the Prophet says that because his rank was higher, he had two devils for him. Two devils were appointed for you. So see, this is a special matter of ranks. The greater the friend of Allah (*wali*), the more intense his devil, his opposition (*zidd*), will be. So, it has always been intense, and until when has it been? Because... the incident of Adam, if you read it, you will know. The first part is not in front of me, otherwise I would have narrated it to you. Qabil... that in the time of Adam, Harith bin Murrah, who... and Allah said to him, "Go, I give respite to your foot soldiers and cavalry, that you do whatever you want to torment my believers." But those who are the followers of truth, who are steadfast on the true religion, they will never be turned back. It will never happen. And in this way, the world has been repeating. Always, before the truth, an opposition has come.

*Yaa ayyuhal ladheena aamanoo anfiqoo mimma razaqnaakum min qabli any ya'tiya yawmul laa bay'un feehi wa laa khullatun wa laa shafaa'ah* (O you who have believed, spend from that which We have provided for you before there comes a Day in which there is no exchange and no friendship and no intercession). O believers, spend from the provision We have given you, before that day comes in which there is neither trade, nor friendship, nor intercession of any kind will be of use. See, this is the variety (*tanawwu'*) of the Holy Quran, that one discussion has finished, just now the story was of Da'ud and his... of Musa, many things happened, and now a new topic has been brought. This variety of the Quran is what creates a special quality in it. Otherwise, if it were the same boring thing, it wouldn't be good. But we always get new topics. Every verse has its own separate place. O believers, the provision We have given you... this is its translation. The provision that has been given, spend from it. And before that day comes, in which there is neither trade, nor friendship, nor intercession of any kind will be of use. This is regarding *zakat*. That *zakat*, which is obligatory on us, whatever our earning is, the fortieth part of it... one rupee in forty, must be given. This is the purification of our wealth.

Meaning, O you who obey the *wasi* and the *imam*! See, this is the translation of the verse, and its *ta'wil* is this. Meaning, O you who obey the *wasi* and the *imam*, benefit others with the esoteric sciences and true gnosis with which We have endowed you. Before that day of resurrection arrives when the *Qa'im* (peace be upon him) appears and brings the *qiyamah* (resurrection), and the transmission of beneficial knowledge from the *a'immah* and *du'at* to the people also ceases and is stopped. Because that is a day of examination and reckoning, not a day of give-and-take and teaching.

*Wal kaafiroona humuz zaalimoon* (And the disbelievers, they are the wrongdoers). The disbelievers are the very wrongdoers. Meaning, those who conceal the stations of the *wasi* and *imam* and do wrong are the wrongdoers. That's it.

Al-Fatiha.

[recitation of Al-Fatiha]

[concluding remarks and sounds of people leaving]

<!-- AUG:ibrahim-2015 lecture 5 -->

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. *Ya ayyuha alladhina amanu anfiqu mimma razaqnakum min qabli an ya'tiya yawmun la bay'un fihi wala khullatun wala shafa'atun.* (O you who have believed, spend from what We have provided for you before there comes a Day in which there is no exchange and no friendship and no intercession.) O believers, spend from the provision We have given you before that day comes in which there will be no exchange, no friendship, and no intercession that will be of any use.

Meaning: O *walī* and followers of the *A'immah* (Imams), We have blessed you with esoteric knowledge (*'ulum-e-batin*) and true gnosis (*ma'rifat-e-haqiqi*); help others benefit from it before the appointed day (*yawm-e-maw'ud*), that is, before the *Qa'im* ('alayhi afdal al-salah) comes and establishes the *Qiyamah*. And the dissemination of knowledge (*ifadah-e-'ilmi*), the call of the Imams (*da'wat al-a'immah*), and intercession (*shafa'at*) will all be suspended, and the command to halt will be given, because that will be the day of examination and reckoning. It will not be a day for transactions or speech.

Wherever *Yawm* (Day) is mentioned... Wherever the word *Yawm* is mentioned, it refers to the Day of Qiyamah. And that is the hour, and in fact, it has been said in other books that *Yawm* refers to the Day of Qiyamah, and *Sa'ah* too... *Sa'at* means hour. The hour is the final hour, which is the time of the *Qa'im* 'alayhi al-salam ('alayhi afdal al-salam). And in one sense, it has been divided from the time of Adam 'alayhi al-salam, and the final hour comes at the time of the *Qa'im* 'alayhi al-salam. So, the meaning of *Sa'ah* is also that, and the meaning of *Yawm* is also the same, that is, the *Qa'im* 'alayhi al-salam. And the Prophet (sallallahu 'alayhi wa-alihi) says that "I and *Sa'at* were walking together, and I went a little ahead." For instance, you said about the two fingers that two fingers are like this, and I have moved one step ahead. Meaning, the sixth *shari'ah*, that of the Prophet (sallallahu 'alayhi wa-alihi), came first, and after that, the *Qa'im* will come. This is the meaning.

*Wal-kafiruna humu al-zalimun.* (And the disbelievers - they are the wrongdoers.) And the disbelievers are the very ones who are the wrongdoers. Meaning, those who conceal the ranks of the *wasī* and the *A'immah* and usurp [their rights] are the wrongdoers. Yesterday, I explained that there is a difference between *mushrikin* (polytheists) and *kuffar* (disbelievers). The *mushrikin* are those who, before Islam, were the people of Arabia who were engrossed in *shirk* (polytheism), and they had many gods that were worshipped. 360 idols were kept in the Kaaba. And in this way, they were involved in all things like fornication and wine-drinking. After the coming of the Prophet, their condition changed, and those *mushrikin* became Muslims. Becoming a Muslim is one thing, and becoming a *mu'min* (believer) is another. The circle of *iman* (faith) began at the time when the Prophet (sallallahu 'alayhi wa-alihi) appointed his successor (*qa'im muqam*), his *khalifah*. The circle of *iman* is a separate matter. Many people had entered Islam before that, in the thousands. "*Yadkhuluna fi dini Allahi afwaja*" (Entering into the religion of Allah in multitudes). O Prophet, a whole army is coming into your religion. But now, among them, how many are good and how many are bad? Because many did not even get the chance to perform deeds. Today they became Muslims, and tomorrow they went into another battle and were martyred. So, in this way, there are many incidents from which it is understood that the circle of Islam is very large, but the circle of *iman* is small.

And in the *da'im al-islam* it has been explained that there is a large circle, and within it, a small circle is made. The one who is in the small circle is also in the large circle, but the one who is in the large circle is not in the small circle. So the one who is a Muslim is not a *mu'min* until he has firm belief in 'Ali. But those who have *iman* are also Muslims. This has been explained.

Now after this, the verses of *tilka al-rusul* which are there, that is Ayat al-Kursi, which we recite daily. Read its translation a bit slowly, so that it remains in people's minds.

The *ta'wil* (esoteric interpretation) of Ayat al-Kursi. *Allahu la ilaha illa hu.* (Allah - there is no deity except Him.) Allah is He besides whom there is no other object of worship. That is, the *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala* (Exalted Precedent) is that very Allah. This lesson... ...No, based on the lesson... Based on... Based on the extreme longing for the *ma'rifat* (gnosis) of the *Mubde'* (Originator)... of the *Mubde'*... of the *Mubde'*, it is either the ultimate... or the ultimate bewilderment of the... See, here, this *Allahu la ilaha illa hu* is actually related to *Shahida Allahu... Ashhadu an la ilaha illa Allah* (Allah witnesses... I bear witness that there is no god but Allah), as we recite. So, *Shahida Allahu annahu la...* Allah has testified that there is no deity except *Hu* (Him). *illa hu*. So, in common language, this is what it means: Allah has testified that there is no god except *Hu*. Well, no one gives their own testimony. Its meaning is that the meaning of *Hu* is separate, and Allah is separate. Allah has testified that there is no deity except *Hu*. And *Hu* is "that one." If you reverse *Hu*, it becomes *woh* ("that one"). "That one" means the one who is in the heavens, the Lord of the Worlds, who is the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala* (Exalted Originator). The day before yesterday, I explained that there is a difference between *Mubde'* and *Mubda'*. If you put a *zer* (vowel mark) under the *dal*, *Mubde'* is the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala* who created the heavens and the earth, who created the spiritual realm. And *Mubda'-e-Awwal* (First Originated), *Mabda'*, that is the *Mubda'*. *Mubda'* is different, *Mubdi'* is different, *Mubda'* is different. *Mubda'* refers to the *'aql-e-awwal* (First Intellect), who was the first to profess the *tawhid* (oneness) of the Lord of the Worlds. And its meaning is, that is, the *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala* (Exalted Precedent). I have explained this before, that *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala* means... The meaning of *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala* is that it refers to the *'aql-e-awwal* (First Intellect). *'Aql-e-awwal*, *mawjud-e-awwal* (first existent), *wujud-e-awwal* (first being), *sabeq-e-awwal* (first precedent)—all these refer to the *'aql-e-awwal*. This is a being who has encompassed the entire universe. Meaning not just the world, but all the realms (*'awalim*). That is why it is *Rabb al-'alamin* (Lord of the worlds), not *Rabb al-'alam* (Lord of the world). *Alhamdulillah Rabb al-'alamin* (All praise is for Allah, Lord of the worlds), meaning there are many realms. How many are there? There is the spiritual realm (*'alam-e-ruhani*), the realm of creation (*'alam-e-khalq*), the physical realm (*'alam-e-jismani*), the psychic realm (*'alam-e-nafsani*), the realm of life (*'alam-e-hayat*). There are many realms. If we go into its details, then where the realm of creation begins, where the realm of intellect (*'alam-e-'aql*) is, where the luminous realm (*'alam-e-nurani*) is... all these things. So, among them, the very first being who professed the *tawhid*... if you don't understand this, you won't understand anything. That the *'aql-e-awwal*... the *ma'dhuniyyat* begins from this very point. That the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala*, who created, created countless beings (*jawads*). He created them all at once. He created them in one go. This is the beginning of the *ma'dhuniyyat*, that Allah Ta'ala created the luminous forms (*ashbah-e-nurani*) in the state of a form (*shibh*), meaning it was a form like the letter *alif*, a human form, but it was luminous, spiritual, and holy. Those beings were countless, meaning there was no count. And only Allah knows the number of His soldiers. How many they were in number. So, the luminous forms were created in a realm, and out of them, the first one to profess the *tawhid* of the Lord of the Worlds was him. And how did he do it? This is the biggest issue and the greatest *tawhid*, that he did it without a teacher (*mu'allim*) and without an inspirer (*mulhim*). Without a teacher. There was no one to explain it to him at that time. Nor was there any *mulhim*, meaning one who gives inspiration. He did it on his own. That is, and look here, it is because of the extreme longing for the gnosis of the *Mubde'*, or extreme bewilderment. Out of bewilderment, he suddenly began to do it, thinking that there must be someone who sustains me. And the first declaration of *tawhid* he made was this: "Neither was I created by myself, nor did I create my own kind. Neither can they create me, nor can I create them. Rather, our creator is someone else."

This is the first sign of *tawhid*. And this *tawhid* which is our special chapter, from which the *ma'dhuniyyat* begins, *tawhid* has three components. One is *tajrid* (abstraction/stripping away), another is *tanzih* (declaring transcendence), and the third is *tawhid*. *Tawhid* means to affirm the oneness of the *Mubde'-e-Subhanahu*, that no one else has the power to create. And for this, what is needed? *Tajrid*. *Jard al-ilahiyyah* (stripping away divinity). *Jard al-ilahiyyah* means he separated all of his own kind from divinity, saying that there is no power in them to create, except for Him. This is called *tajrid*. *Jard*. And there are many meanings to it, I don't want to go into the details now. *Tanzih*. The second word is *tanzih*. *Munazzah 'an al-sifat* (transcendent above attributes). He separated the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala*, who created the spiritual realm, from attributes. He stripped him, separated him, made him transcendent. Making him transcendent means from attributes. Attributes apply to created beings, not to Allah. So the Lord of the Worlds is far above this. Now the *Asma' al-Husna* (The Most Beautiful Names) that we recite, Ya Allah, Ya Rahman, Ya Rahim, all these attributes are all attributes. So we are told from the beginning that these are all names of Allah. It's correct. But these are not the names of Allah, rather they are of Allah's special angels (*mala'ikah*) upon whom these names are bestowed. How so? For example, Ya Qadir, Ya Ghafur, Ya Rahim. All of these... *Ghafur* means the forgiver, *Sattar* means the concealer of faults. So you, even a small... you too have concealed the faults of many people. You have forgiven many people. So you have become *Ghafur*? So you are also *Ghafur* and Allah is also *Ghafur*? This cannot be. That is why the name we give is... we never give the name Ghaffar. It is *'Abd al-Ghaffar*. It is *'Abd al-Qadir*. All of Allah's 99 names, *'abd* (servant) comes with them. Names like Qadir and so on are not given. It is *'Abd al-Qadir*, *'Abd al-Ra'uf*—all these names are given. So the meaning is that *munazzah 'an al-sifat* (transcendent of attributes)... from attributes... stripping him of attributes. This is the second thing. And the *tawhid* of *Rabb al-'alamin*, that there is no deity but Him.

And this is... what did he say? Now here, the meaning of *Allahu la ilaha illa hu* is *Shahida Allahu annahu la ilaha illa hu* (Allah has witnessed that there is no deity except Him). I bear witness that... meaning Allah has witnessed. *Shahida Allahu... la ilaha illa hu.* That there is no deity except *Hu*. That *Hu* is... the meaning of *Hu* is that strange scene where there was nothing, from non-existence, from *'adam*, He brought it into existence. And he created the luminous forms in thousands, lakhs, crores, countless, innumerable, and immeasurable beings.

So remember that the spiritual realm is a special one, which is also called the realm of intellect (*'alam-e-'aql*). It is also called the spiritual realm. And after that, the realms that exist, their names are different. From where the division has occurred, from where the matter of believing came. Now look, this thing is that whatever we are reading is this: *Allahu la ilaha illa hu*. Allah is He besides whom there is no other object of worship. That is, the *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala* (Exalted Precedent). Who is the *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala*? The *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala* is He. He is *Ta'ala* (exalted), very high. He is far above us. He is the first veil (*hijab-e-awwal*). He became the veil of the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala*. The moment he professed the *tawhid* of *Rabb al-'alamin*, he became distinct from all the realms, and he was elevated. And his state was such that it was so... I mean, first he was a luminous form, and then the light began to increase. It increased and increased so much that those below fell into bewilderment. In seeing him, they became bewildered. And from that, the guidance, they say, and the *ma'dhuniyyat* which begins, it starts from this very thing. That the beings below, however many there were, thousands and lakhs, at that moment their gaze fell, "Oh, who is this whose light has become more than ours? He was just like us." So, the *'aql-e-awwal*... now people are bewildered about him. So in one sense, the meaning in our tradition is that the *'aql-e-awwal* himself has been called Allah. So Allah is... the meaning of Allah, look in the *Munjid*, look in the dictionary, the meaning of Allah is that about whom one feels longing (*shawq*) or about whom one is bewildered.

So look here, its meaning is the extreme longing for the *ma'rifat* (gnosis) of the *Mubde'*, or the state of being drowned in extreme bewilderment. What is it? It is Allah, derived from *waliha*. Allah is derived from *waliha* or from *aliha*. Or from *aliha* or from *yaliuhu*. Because... ...the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala*, neither can any indication be made towards Him, nor can any attribute be applied to Him. The prayer that we offer, all of it is offered to the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala*. It is not for anyone else. Meaning, among the created beings, from the *'aql-e-awwal* down to here, the prayer is not for anyone. We know, in terms of association, that today is this day, this is for Mawlana 'Ali, this is for the Prophet, this month is like this. But the prayer that is offered is only for the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala*. If it is directed away from Him, it is not a prayer at all. So remember that the one who is the true master, the Lord of the Worlds, the creator of the spiritual realm, He is the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala*, for whom the word *Hu* has been used. And only an indication can be made. Look, it is an indication. So no indication can be made... no kind of indication we can make that He is in the sky, on the earth, He is not here, He is in the valley, He is not on the mountain. It cannot be anything. Wherever He is, there Allah is. So remember this, that Allah is *Khabir* (All-Aware) and '*Alim* (All-Knowing). The entire Quran is filled with this, that Allah Ta'ala is also *'Alim*, He is also *Basir* (All-Seeing), everything is in Him. So what this means is that all the names we take, the *Asma' al-Husna*, they are Allah's special representatives. Now, Ya Qadir, Ya Ra'uf, Ya Ghafur, these names that are being taken, which beings do they apply to? We don't have knowledge of that. So the *du'at* say that if you don't know the specifics, then understand that all these things apply to the *'aql-e-awwal*. That the *'aql-e-awwal* is that being who encompasses everything. And above him is only the *Mubde'-e-Ta'ala*, who created the spiritual realm. Neither can our thoughts reach him... these are the sentences of the oath. The oath that is taken, its secrets are given. That neither can our inner sight (*basirat*) reach him, nor our outer sight (*basarat*), nor can our thoughts reach him. We cannot see with our eyes. We can only believe. And how do we believe? Well, *ma'rifat* (gnosis) is not possible. The *ma'rifat* of Allah, the *Mubde'-e-Subhanahu*, who created the spiritual realm and the... the spiritual realm... how can we have *ma'rifat* of him? So for *ma'rifat*, it has been said that *ma'rifat* is simply not possible. Just be helpless. *La tujasirunnahu al-khawatir.* (Thoughts do not venture towards Him.) Our hearts cannot have notions. No thought can enter our hearts. Remember this, that we can believe. So what is there for that? That we should just affirm. That He is, that's all. And point the finger and say *Shahida Allahu annahu la ilaha illa hu.* (Allah has witnessed that there is no deity except Him.)

*Al-Hayy* (The Ever-Living). Alive. *Al-Hayy*, He is alive. This is an indication towards the *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala* who is the source of eternal life. Now look, all the attributes are for him, they are being applied to the *Mubda'-e-Awwal*, and they are for the *Sabeq-e-Awwal*. *Al-Hayy*, He is alive. This is an indication towards the *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala*, who is the source of eternal life. All life forms emerged from him. In recognizing him... "Hey, these were our companions, how did he become so luminous?" And it has been said that the light that started to form in them... the light and radiance... in the revelation... kept increasing. It increased so much that the people below were bewildered. They became so low that he became the *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala*, and he was given the rank of *'aql-e-awwal*. Now look, in the realm of intellects, you all keep reading the *ma'dhuniyyat*, you know it happens every year. This *'aql-e-awwal* has gone up to the tenth intellect (*'aql-e-'ashir*). *'Aql* (intellect)... the meaning of *'aql* is "to tie." To tie, to keep something in control. The camel that is there, a stake is driven for the camel, and it is tied with a rope, and the camel can only roam in the area it covers in a circle. It cannot go beyond that. So that is called *'aql*. So this *'aql* is... that *'aql*, if our intellect works, it will work only according to our understanding. If we show excessive cleverness, we will become foolish. So remember, the intellect has a restraint. That is why it has been said that those who act presumptuously, they become foolish and do not keep their intellect in check.

*Al-Qayyum* (The Self-Sustaining). He is established. Meaning... *Al-Hayy al-Qayyum*. He is also *Qayyum*. He is established. Meaning all the realms... ...the helper of all the realms. Now, all the support (*ta'idat*), whatever we understand, that we are getting support, where is it from? We get support from the *Da'i* of the time. Where does the *Da'i* of the time get it from? He says from the Imam. Where does the Imam get it from? He says from the *Qawa'im*. Where do the *Qawa'im* get it from? From the *Jusus*. Where do the *Jusus* get it from? From above. It goes on and on up to the *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala*, and it is the *Sabeq-e-Ta'ala* who is the helper of all. The one who gives help is him. Meaning, the helper of all the realms is him.

*Wala ta'khudhuhu sinatun wala nawm.* (Neither drowsiness nor sleep overtakes Him.) *La ta'khudhuhu sinatun wala nawm.* *La ta'khudhuhu sinatun wala nawm.* Neither... Now look, we recite this daily, five times we recite it. And what is the verse? *La ta'khudhuhu sinatun wala nawm.* Neither does drowsiness overcome him, nor sleep. We fall asleep while listening to the lecture. The lecture is going on, and it's a human nature that you listen to a lecture for some time, and then your mind gets tired, and you fall asleep. This has been observed. So what is the matter? Neither does sleep ever overcome him, nor... a jolt. Drowsiness. Drowsiness comes. Meaning, in giving help... ...laziness does not come to him, nor... Now look, the meaning... its real *ta'wil* (esoteric interpretation) is. This we were just reading the translation, that neither drowsiness overcomes him, nor sleep. Meaning, he never shows laziness in giving help. The one who is receiving it, is receiving it from there. It is coming from above.

*Lahu ma fi al-samawati wa ma fi al-ard.* (To Him belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth.) The heavens and the earth, whatever is in them, is His. Yes. Meaning... the manifest and the hidden, He is the master of it all. Yes. Now see, this has many meanings. Two or three lines of the translation are coming. That He is the master of the manifest (*zahir*) and the hidden (*batin*). He is of the heavens and the earth. The heavens, meaning the seven heavens, and the seven earths have been created. Adam, Nuh, Ibrahim, Musa, 'Isa, and the Prophet, and our coming *Qa'im*, who is the seventh. So these seven *Nutuqa'* are in the station of the heavens, and their *Wasīs* are in the station of the earth. And it is also related to the noble *da'wat*. Two meanings. The heavens and the earth, whatever is in them, is His. Meaning, of the manifest and the hidden. You have been hearing from two perspectives, in the morning lesson and in the evening lesson, that He is the master of the manifest (*zahir*) and the hidden (*batin*). The manifest is a separate thing, the *tanzil* (exoteric revelation), and the *ta'wil* (esoteric interpretation), He is the master of both.

*Man dhalladhi...* *Man dhalladhi yashfa'u 'indahu illa bi-idhnihi.* (Who is it that can intercede with Him except by His permission?) Whose power is it that without His permission... Whose power is it that without His permission, one can intercede in His presence?

Meaning... His *Bab-e-Ta'li* (Gate of the Follower), besides him... no one can intercede. Look, *Bab*. There is *Bab*, and above it, *Ta'li* is also written. The *Ta'li* is the same... the *Ta'li* is the being of the *Munba'ith-e-Awwal* (First Emissary). That if... where... you have read the *ma'dhuniyyat*, you have seen it. I don't know if the *ma'dhuniyyat* has happened here or not. But when the *'aql-e-awwal* was established in his rank, the veil of... he became the veil of God. He became the greatest veil. At that time, his joy and his... was such that his radiance increased so much that the people below fell into bewilderment. Now, in that, it has been explained that only two individuals moved forward. This is a special topic of the *ma'dhuniyyat*, it is a topic of *tawhid*, that when the *'aql-e-awwal* took the lead, after that, two individuals arose, two forms arose. And in that, one is the *Munba'ith-e-Awwal* and one is the *Munba'ith-e-Thani*. *Munba'ith* means the one who rises, rising. One is the *Munba'ith-e-Awwal* and one is the *Munba'ith-e-Thani*. Two arose. Now, the meaning of two arising is that even today, if you look at numbers, after one, it is always two that comes. Take out all the books, wherever... all the languages in the world, all of them, after the singular, the plural comes. After one, the plural form comes. Except for Arabic, in which there is also a dual (*tathniyah*). *Tathniyah* means two, dual. So what this means is that the greatest system of Allah was that of the *'aql-e-awwal*, and after the *'aql-e-awwal*, two forms arose. And both of them tried. And how did they try? That... neither do we, from ourselves... create all these, nor do they create us. They first affirmed one thing. The second is that he preceded us in this... in professing *tawhid*. They acknowledged the rank of the *'aql-e-awwal*. They affirmed it. That both of them did it. Both of them, if you read, you will find out that both of them affirmed that the *'aql-e-awwal* is better than us. And we are moving forward by looking at him. So see, the first one was without a teacher, without an inspirer. Here, both of them moved forward by looking at him, in the *tawhid* of God. This is the second chapter.

But what was the difference? From here, see, the separation began. The system of the world that was disrupted and the opposites that were created below, they were created for this very reason. What happened? That among them, one did not pay heed to the second. The two who had risen, one's gaze was lofty and one's was base. So the lofty one was... the second person... both had risen in rank. But the rank was... he was given one, and the second one, his... had a delusion of equality (*wahm-e-masawat*). "I am equal to him." The one above is bestowing a gaze on someone, and you are saying I am also... This is what happened there. And what happened? The *Munba'ith-e-Awwal*, who was the first... whose gaze was lofty, and the gaze... the meaning of gaze is very deep, chapter after chapter is on it. How much can be explained? That his gaze was, but the second one said that I believe in Allah, I believe in this *'aql-e-awwal* who is superior. But I don't believe in him, because he was with me, we are both equal. Just this... the delusion... and that is why in Sayyidna Ghulam's book, if you read, you will find out that delusion, envy, greed, all these are bad habits. They are the "mothers of sins" (*ummahat ma'asi*). The sin is... which is a sin, among them, this is also one. To have a delusion. Delusion that we are both one. This is wrong. No one is equal. "*Faddalna ba'dahum 'ala ba'd*" (We have preferred some of them over others). Each one is given preference over another. So what this means is that there, the second intellect, the mistake it made was this, that "I and he are equal." As soon as... he stagnated. He stood still. He stopped. And the *Munba'ith-e-Awwal* became the veil of the *'aql-e-awwal*. When this happened, a change occurred in the realm. You read in the *ma'dhuniyyat* the subjects and its... listen to the cassette, you will find out. That in the two, one became superior, who is called the *Munba'ith-e-Awwal*. Now the *Munba'ith-e-Thani* stopped. But the realm was a realm of liberty, a realm of freedom. At that time, all the intellects moved forward. And how many were there? Seven intellects moved forward. Seven intellects moved forward, and seven and these two became nine. And after that, the one who was the *Munba'ith-e-Thani*, whose gaze was base, he realized that "I was up there, how did I come here?" And at that very moment, he thought that "No, I have to ask." So the one who was the eighth... ninth form, the ninth intellect, in the circle of the intellect, there were thousands of people, thousands of forms. He asked the last person of the ninth circle, "What is my mistake? I was there, how did I come here?" At that time, his request was forwarded upwards. And it reached the *Munba'ith-e-Awwal*. And he said that this mistake was of equality. It was the delusion of equality. As soon as he found out, he repented. Look, repentance... he repented. And as soon as he repented, his repentance was accepted, and he became the tenth intellect (*'aqil-e-'ashir*). The nine intellects had already been formed, this is the tenth intellect. And he is the tenth intellect who created the entire world.
